## LIME-10-fix12 — Send inline with input, simplified toolbar toggle

- **This brief's HTML restructure resolves a tension flagged (and worked around) three times already**: LIME-10-fix3 through fix9 all had to keep `.lime-composer__toolbar` as a container that could never be `display: none`, because Send lived inside it and had to stay visible collapsed — so visibility was faked per-child instead (`.lime-composer__tool`/`.lime-composer__divider` individually toggled). Moving Send into the new `.lime-composer__row` (alongside the textarea) removes the reason for that workaround entirely: the toolbar container can now genuinely be `display: none` as a whole. Removed the per-child toggle rules and the conditional padding-top/margin-top gating that existed only to work around the old constraint — they're unconditional again now, same as any other element that's simply not rendered at all when collapsed.
- Textarea changed `flex: none` → `flex: 1` (needs to grow and fill the row now that it has a sibling, Send, instead of being the box's sole child) and dropped `width: 100%` (would have fought `flex: 1` and overflowed past Send in the same row) in favor of `min-width: 0`, the standard flex-child pattern for something that needs to shrink freely alongside a fixed-size sibling.
- Removed the now-dead `.lime-composer__spacer` CSS rule along with its markup — it existed only to push Send to the right end of the old toolbar row; with Send relocated, nothing references it anymore.
- "Grows upward on expand" needed no new code: `.lime-composer` is `position: absolute; bottom: 0;` with auto height — an element pinned to its container's bottom edge always grows upward as its content gets taller, inherent to that positioning, not something to implement.
- Item 6 (`.lime-nav-dropdown__item:hover` background + cursor) was already fully present on the *base* rule; added the literal duplicate to `:hover` anyway per the brief's explicit ask, but couldn't identify or reproduce an actual "broken hover" defect via static code review, and had no way to test live interaction without screen access. Flagged for the user to point out more specifically if this doesn't resolve what they were seeing.
- Verified via source inspection only (brace/tag balance, confirmed `app.js` references `#composer-input`/`#composer-send` purely by id — unaffected by which parent element now wraps them) and opened in Firefox for the user's visual gate check; no automated in-browser assertions were possible (see the environment note above this entry).


## LIME-10-fix13 — Tighter spacing, smaller aux icons, scroll-on-grow, code icon fix

- **`.lime-messages` padding-bottom is a reasoned estimate, not a measurement, flagged as such**: LIME-10-fix5 calibrated this (220px) against a live-measured 182px composer height. LIME-10-fix9 and fix12 both shrank the collapsed composer significantly since (conditional toolbar padding, then Send moved out of the toolbar entirely), so 220px was stale — but without Chrome available for `getBoundingClientRect()` checks (Firefox has no scripting interface this sandbox can read from), I couldn't re-measure the way every prior composer-height fix in this project did. Reduced to 150px by manual CSS arithmetic (~100-110px estimated actual height + a generous buffer), erring toward "too much clearance" rather than risk the clipping regression already fixed twice. This one specifically needs the user's own visual confirmation.
- `.lime-composer__voice .dew-microphone`'s own specific override (16px, from LIME-10-fix12) would have kept winning over a plain change to the general `.lime-composer__aux .dew` rule (both are 2-class-selector specificity ties; the more specific rule was declared later) — updated both to 14px so the mic icon actually shrinks alongside the plus icon, not just the rule that looked like the "obvious" one to touch.
- `.lime-composer__actions`'s `padding-top` (4px, since LIME-10-fix8) was adding on top of `.lime-composer`'s own `gap` (4px) between the box and actions rows — effectively 8px of combined spacing where only one of the two was likely intended. Removed the redundant `padding-top`, leaving the composer's own `gap` as the sole source of that spacing.
- Code button's `</>` was overflowing/wrapping inside the 32px circular button at 18px font — no `white-space` was set, and three characters at that size don't reliably fit one line in that width. Added `white-space: nowrap` (kept the existing `flex-shrink: 0` from LIME-10-fix8, which alone doesn't stop text from wrapping).
- Items 4 (`.lime-composer__row` `align-items: flex-end`) and 5 (Send `align-self: flex-end`, redundant with item 4's own default but added per the brief's literal ask) — item 4 was already exactly this from LIME-10-fix12; added item 5's explicit restatement anyway since it's harmless.
- Scroll-on-grow: the textarea's existing auto-grow `input` handler (LIME-10) didn't rescroll `.lime-messages` — as the box eats into the thread's space from the bottom while growing, the latest message would slide out of view without an explicit rescroll. Added `thread.scrollTop = thread.scrollHeight` to the same handler.
- Verified via source inspection only (brace balance across both CSS files, `app.js` syntax check) — no live browser introspection was available this round (see the LIME-10-fix12 entry's environment note). Opened in Firefox for the user's visual gate check; the padding-bottom estimate above specifically needs their eyes, not just mine.


## LIME-10-fix14 — Scrollbar styling, Send disabled state, corner-anchored position

- **Item 2 (Send "moves into the toolbar row when expanded") isn't something the brief's own scope allows literally**: it describes Send living in two different DOM parents depending on state, but the brief's scope only lists `lime.css`/`app.js` — no `index.html`, so no markup change (like duplicating the button) was expected. Used `position: absolute` on `.lime-composer__return`, anchored to `.lime-composer__box` (given `position: relative`) at a fixed corner (`right`/`bottom` matching the box's own padding). Since the box's height is auto and this is the only thing pinned to its corner, whichever row currently ends up last — the input row alone when collapsed, or input row + toolbar once expanded — sits flush against it either way, without the button ever actually moving in the DOM. Reserved matching space (`padding-right: 40px`) on both the textarea and the toolbar so their own content doesn't render underneath the now out-of-flow button.
- Double-checked the absolute-positioning offset convention before using it: the containing block for an absolutely positioned element is its ancestor's *padding-box* edge, not its content edge — `right: 0` would sit the button flush against the border, ignoring the box's own padding entirely. Used `right: var(--seed-space-3); bottom: var(--seed-space-2)` (matching the box's actual padding values) instead, so the button lines up with where padding-respecting content already sits.
- `--soil-bg-subtle` (item 3's requested active-state background) doesn't exist — same gap found and substituted the same way in LIME-10-fix11. Used `--calm-bg-subtle-default`.
- **Real bug caught before it shipped, not just the brief's literal ask**: `handleSend` clears the textarea via `composerInput.value = ''` directly, which does not fire a native `input` event — so the new `.is-active` toggle (wired to that event) would never see the post-send clear on its own, leaving Send looking "active" (solid) right after a message was sent, until the next keystroke. Added an explicit `composerSend.classList.remove('is-active')` inside `handleSend` itself rather than relying on the event.
- Verified via source inspection only (brace balance, `app.js` syntax check, confirmed `composerSend` was already an existing `const` in `handleSend`'s closure) — no live positioning check was possible in this sandbox (see the LIME-10-fix12/fix13 environment notes). The corner-anchored positioning in particular is unverified pixel-for-pixel and needs the user's own eyes at the gate.


## LIME-11 — Reply Threads

- **This brief brought the reply panel into scope for the first time since LIME-06** — every brief in between explicitly excluded it ("do not touch reply panel"), so this is the first real look at it since then.
- **Real, pre-existing dead code found during survey**: the old "Reply thread panel" JS listened for a click on `#open-replies` — an id that was deleted from the markup back in LIME-06, when the hardcoded thread messages it lived on were removed. Its early-return guard (`if (!openReplies) return`) has silently fired on every page load since, meaning the back-to-profile button never worked either, despite still existing in the markup. Rebuilt entirely as a delegated click on `#thread-messages .lime-message__actions [title="Reply"]` — delegation, not the old one-time `forEach`, so it keeps working after switching conversations replaces `#thread-messages`'s content wholesale, unlike the old approach would have even if its target still existed.
- **A second real, pre-existing bug found and fixed, beyond this brief's literal ask but required for "replies show avatars" to actually work**: `paintAvatar` (initials + color for `.lime-avatar[data-name]`) only ever ran once, in a one-time `querySelectorAll().forEach()` at parse time. Any avatar added afterward — every message sent via the main composer since LIME-07, and now every reply — rendered as a blank circle with no initials or color, since nothing re-ran that logic. Promoted it to a reusable top-level function; `handleSend` (LIME-07) and the new reply-rendering code both now call it explicitly on their own newly-inserted avatars.
- Seed data reality check before building anything: only one real reply existed in the entire dataset (`msg-004` → `msg-003`), despite `msg-003` itself claiming `reply_count: 4`. Added 2 more sample replies (`msg-1004`, `msg-1005`) to `seed-data/messages.json`, keeping them from the only two people who could realistically be in that thread — `conv-001` is a 1:1 DM between just Jean Chung and Shem Robinson, so a reply from anyone else (the old hardcoded panel's "Mary Lee"/"Valene Rajoon" weren't even participants in that conversation) would have been the exact same coherence bug flagged back in LIME-04a. Regenerated the embedded `public/js/seed-data.js` mirror from the updated source, per the process established in LIME-06. `reply_count`/`last_reply_at` are treated as decorative/stale metadata, never trusted for a displayed count — `getRepliesForMessage()` always computes the real list from actual `reply_to` relationships, the same principle already applied to reactions in LIME-08.
- Reply items use a new, distinct `.lime-reply` class (avatar/sender/time/text only) rather than reusing `.lime-message` wholesale — reversing a LIME-03s decision, per this brief's explicit ask for something "smaller/compact," and because real replies don't carry reactions or a react/reply/more actions bar the way a thread message does.
- Reply composer reuses the exact same `.lime-composer__box`/`.lime-composer__row`/`.lime-composer__toolbar`/`.lime-composer__tool`/`.lime-composer__return` classes as the main composer (built across LIME-09 through LIME-10-fix14) — same single-line/expand/Send-disabled-until-typed behavior for free. Added `#replies-composer .lime-composer__box { width: 100% }` to override the main composer's `75%` (tuned for a wide centered chat panel, not this narrow sidebar), and extended the two `#composer.is-expanded` selectors to also match `#replies-composer.is-expanded` rather than duplicating their property values. The expand/collapse/auto-grow/is-active JS logic itself is a second, separate block, not a shared function — this brief's scope explicitly excludes touching the main composer, and merging the two would mean editing that already-working code.
- Promoted `presenceFor`/`shortName`/`escapeHtml`/`formatTime`/`formatDay`/`formatDuration`/`previewFor` out of the LIME-06 closure to top-level scope so the new reply code could reuse them — all were already pure, parameter-only functions with no dependency on that closure's own state, so this changes nothing about how the existing contacts/thread code uses them. Added a new `plainPreviewFor()` (text-only, no icon markup) for the reply quote, rather than stripping HTML out of `previewFor`'s output.
- **Known characteristic, not a new bug**: replies (both the existing `msg-004` and the 2 new sample ones) also appear in the main thread's own flat scroll, since `getMessagesByConversation` was already returning every message in a conversation regardless of `reply_to` — this predates this brief. Left as-is; filtering replies out of the main thread would be a bigger, unrequested change to already-shipped behavior.
- **Not fixed, flagged only**: the profile-opening click handler (`.lime-message__sender, .lime-message .lime-avatar` → open profile panel) has the exact same one-time-`forEach` staleness bug `paintAvatar` had — it only wires up whichever messages existed at page-load time, so clicking a name/avatar in a conversation switched to *after* load, or on a freshly-sent message, does nothing. Real, but unrelated to this brief's goal and not required for it — named here rather than silently fixed, since fixing it wasn't asked for.
- Verified via source inspection only — brace/tag balance across all three touched files, `app.js`/`data.js` syntax checks, confirmed the `getComputedStyle`-style introspection isn't available in this sandbox (Firefox preview only, no scripting access). This is the largest, least-verified change of the session; opened in Firefox for the user's own click-through of the full flow (open replies, see the 3-message seeded thread, type and send a new reply, back button, switching to a message with no replies yet).


## LIME-11-fix2 — Avatars/timestamp/reactions/full composer on replies

- Item 1 ("add avatars to replies") was already fully implemented in LIME-11 — `replyHtml()` already sets `data-name` on a `.seed-avatar--sm` and `renderReplies()` already calls `paintAvatar()` on every one. No code change made; couldn't reproduce or identify an actual defect via static review. Flagged for the user to point out more specifically if avatars still aren't showing after this update, same as the LIME-10-fix14 dropdown-hover note.
- Removed `#replies-back` (the chevron button) from the markup entirely per this brief's explicit ask — the only way out of the replies view now is the panel's own top-level close button, not a "back to profile" step. This is a real, deliberate loss of a navigation path, not an oversight; implemented literally since the brief asked for exactly this.
- **Real layout bug caught before it rendered wrong**: `.lime-replies-panel__composer` was `display: flex` with no `flex-direction` set (defaulting to row) — harmless while it only ever held one child (the old plain input pill), but this brief adds a second child (`.lime-composer__actions`, item 6), which would have rendered side-by-side with the box instead of stacked beneath it. Added `flex-direction: column`.
- Reactions on the quote and reply items required generalizing the existing reaction-picker delegate, not just adding markup: it was hardcoded to `.closest('.lime-message')` in three places. Introduced a shared `REACTABLE` selector (`.lime-message, .lime-reply, .lime-replies-panel__quote`) and swapped all three lookups to use it. This also let a genuinely dead `if (!messageId)` fallback branch get deleted — it existed for the reply panel's old hardcoded messages (no real message object to look up), but LIME-11 already removed those; every reactable container now always has a real `data-message-id`.
- Reply composer's actions row (Attach / voice+chevron / privacy text) reuses the exact same classes as the main composer, with its own unique ids (`replies-voice-mode-toggle`/`-dropdown`, since duplicate ids are invalid HTML) and its own `#replies-composer .lime-composer__actions { width: 100% }` override — same reasoning as LIME-11's existing `.lime-composer__box` override, since the main composer's `75%` was tuned for the wide centered chat panel, not this narrow sidebar.
- Promoted `REACTION_PICKER_HTML`/`reactionsHtml`/`renderReactions` out of the LIME-06 closure to top-level scope, alongside the helpers already promoted in LIME-11 — same reasoning: pure functions with no dependency on that closure's own state, needed by the reply panel's separate closure.
- Added a live "X replies · last reply TIME" line below the quote, computed from `getRepliesForMessage()` every time the panel opens — like reactions and the reply list itself, never trusts the seed message's own stale `reply_count`/`last_reply_at` fields.
- **Known limitation, not fixed**: reacting to the quote (visible in the right panel) doesn't live-update that same message's bubble in the main thread if it's simultaneously visible in the center panel (or vice versa) — `renderReactions()` only touches the one DOM element that was actually clicked, not every element sharing that message's id. Would need to fix by re-rendering all matching elements, not the code path yet.
- Verified via source inspection only (brace/tag balance, `app.js`/`data.js` syntax checks) — no live introspection available. Opened in Firefox for the user's visual gate check; this is the second largest, least-verified change of the session after LIME-11 itself.


## LIME-11-fix3 — Scroll to bottom on conversation open

- Verified before touching anything: `renderThread()` — used for both the initial page-load render of the default conversation and every subsequent conversation switch, since both paths call this same function — never scrolled at all, always leaving the view at the top of the thread. Matches this brief's premise exactly, unlike several earlier "fix" briefs this session that described already-corrected state.
- Item 3 (scroll after sending) was already in place from LIME-07, confirmed unchanged rather than re-added.
- Added `thread.scrollTop = thread.scrollHeight` at the end of `renderThread()`, after the message-rendering loop — the empty-conversation branch (`msgs.length === 0`) returns before reaching it, so an empty thread is correctly left alone (nothing to scroll to).
- Brief's own gate was "No — behavioral check only"; no browser check performed for this one.


## LIME-12 — Fix Mobile Nav

- **Surveyed before touching anything, since this area predates this session's own work**: the mobile drawer's actual implementation lives mostly in `vendor/seed/components/layout/layout.css`, not `lime.css` — Lime only adds the trigger button + its own backdrop (`.lime-mobile-nav-backdrop`), reusing Seed's `.seed-layout--mobile-open` mechanism for the panel itself (real class name; the brief's guessed `.seed-layout--mobile-nav-open` doesn't exist).
- **Root cause identified, not guessed**: Seed's own mobile treatment for this panel is a floating, *inset* card (`top/left/bottom: 16px`, not flush against any edge) toggled via `display: none` ↔ `flex` — and `display` changes can never be animated by a CSS transition, full stop. That combination (inset on every side, zero slide animation) is very plausibly what read as "floating icons over content, not a proper drawer" — not a separate bug causing individual icons to render disconnected. Checked for one anyway: nothing in this app's own sidebar/nav CSS sets `position: fixed` or `absolute`, and Seed's own unused `.seed-layout__reveal-*`/`.seed-layout__backdrop` rules are inert since Lime's markup never includes those elements. No stray-positioned elements exist to hide (item 5's ask), and the JS toggle (item 4) already correctly adds/removes the right classes on hamburger/backdrop/Escape — verified against the actual code, not assumed, before deciding no JS change was needed.
- Implemented items 1/2/4 as: keep the panel always "displayed" (`display: flex`, not Seed's `display: none` at rest) but parked off-screen at `left: -100%`, sliding to `left: 0` on `.seed-layout--mobile-open` — `left` *can* animate, unlike `display`, so `transition: left 0.2s ease` (item 1's own ask) actually does something now.
- **Real specificity bug caught and fixed before it shipped as a half-working hybrid**: Seed's own `.seed-layout--mobile-open .seed-layout__left` rule is 2-class specificity — higher than a single-class override rule, so it would have kept winning for every property except the one I explicitly restated (`left`), regardless of source order. A first draft that only overrode `left` would have produced a genuine hybrid: edge-to-edge horizontally (from the new rule) but still inset top/bottom at Seed's old 16px values and the old, narrower `min(260px, 75vw)` width (from Seed's rule, since specificity beats source order). Fixed by restating every property (`display`/`position`/`top`/`bottom`/`width`/`z-index`) in the override, not just the one this brief happened to mention.
- Left `.lime-mobile-nav-backdrop`'s existing overlay color (`rgba(19, 27, 23, 0.3)`, Seed's own "soil" dark tone) unchanged rather than replacing it with the brief's literal `rgba(0,0,0,0.3)` — the current value already matches Seed's own backdrop treatment used consistently elsewhere in this app (the search modal's backdrop, and Seed's own inert `.seed-layout__backdrop` rule), and swapping to a generic black would have been the one inconsistent backdrop color in the app.
- Attempted to verify by resizing the Firefox window to a mobile-narrow width via AppleScript before opening the page — the command ran without error, but with no way to read back window state or take a screenshot in this sandbox, I have no way to confirm whether it actually took effect. This brief's own gate ("test on mobile viewport") genuinely needs the user's own device/browser check; nothing here was verified visually.


## LIME-12-fix (unnumbered) — Real z-index bug from the screenshot

- User reported "menu still broken when resized" with a screenshot: the whole mobile contacts view washed out gray, and one isolated, otherwise-unexplained avatar circle floating near the bottom of the screen with no visible drawer content around it.
- **Found the real cause by mapping every z-index in play, not by guessing**: LIME-12's drawer used `z-index: 100` (the brief's own literal suggested value, copied without checking it against this app's real z-index scale). `.lime-mobile-nav-backdrop` already uses `var(--seed-z-overlay)` = 300 — 3x higher. Whenever the drawer opened, the backdrop rendered *on top of* it, its semi-transparent dark tint washing out the drawer's lower-contrast content (icons, text on a card background) while the one element with a solid, higher-contrast fill color (the user avatar's pastel background) still read through faintly — fully explaining both symptoms in the screenshot with one bug, not two.
- Changed both drawer rules' `z-index` from the literal `100` to `var(--seed-z-modal)` (400, a real token one tier above the backdrop's 300) rather than another arbitrary literal number.
- Also removed Seed's base panel styling (rounded corners + card shadow) from the mobile drawer specifically — not reported as broken, but a flush edge-to-edge drawer with rounded corners produces a visible notch where the corner meets the screen edge, directly undermining this brief's own "proper drawer" goal if left in.
- Still no way to visually confirm in this sandbox — asked the user to re-check on their own device/window after this fix.


## LIME-12-fix2 — Redesigned as a "push" reveal, not an overlay

- User's screenshot showed the earlier z-index fix made zero visible difference — same gray wash, same floating avatar. Rather than guess a third time from screenshots, used Chrome briefly as a pure internal diagnostic (never shown as a preview) to read actual computed styles: confirmed the backdrop was NOT open, `body`'s real background was a normal light cream with no filter/opacity anywhere. Asked the user a direct diagnostic question (does the tint show on other sites too) to rule out a browser extension — they confirmed it's page-specific, and used the answer to redirect entirely: they don't want an overlay-with-backdrop drawer at all. They want the sidebar to reveal in its own space and push the center panel over, matching the desktop collapse/expand feel minus the icon-only state.
- **Full model change, not a bug fix**: reverted LIME-12's `position: fixed` overlay entirely. `.seed-layout__left` goes back to being a normal grid item; the grid's own `grid-template-columns` now animates `0px 1fr` → `280px 1fr` on `.seed-layout--mobile-open`, so opening the nav actually resizes the layout (pushing `.seed-layout__center` into the second column) rather than floating on top of it. No backdrop at all — `.lime-mobile-nav-backdrop` is force-hidden at this breakpoint, since a push model has no overlay region to dim or tap-to-close.
- Deliberately used `0px 1fr` for the closed state instead of Seed's own bare `1fr` — `grid-template-columns` only animates reliably between values with the *same number of tracks*; changing track count (1 vs 2) doesn't interpolate consistently across browsers, only changing a track's size does.
- **Real bug caught via live introspection, not guessed**: `#layout` can carry a stale `seed-layout--collapsed-left` class left over from earlier desktop interaction (persisted via `localStorage`), and that class's `min-width: 56px` rule (unconditional, 2-class specificity) kept beating the plain mobile `min-width: 0` reset — forcing the "closed" column to 56px instead of 0px even though the grid template itself said `0px`. Added a matching-specificity override so the collapsed-left leftover can never interfere at this breakpoint (a mobile user should never see that state at all — its own toggle is already hidden here).
- **Second real bug caught via live introspection**: even after that fix, `.seed-layout__center` was measured landing in grid column 1 — the same horizontal position as `.seed-layout__left`, visibly overlapping it — despite neither element having any `grid-column`, `order`, or `display` override that should cause that (checked all three directly against the live DOM, not assumed). Rather than keep chasing why auto-placement was doing this, gave both panels an explicit `grid-column` (1 and 2) to sidestep it regardless of the underlying cause. Reverified after: center panel's left edge moved from 0 to 296 (280px column + 16px gap) — confirmed via the same live-introspection technique, not assumed fixed.
- **Known minor cosmetic gap, not chased further**: the open sidebar measures 260px wide, not the intended 280px (its grid column is correctly 280px; something knocks 20px off the element's own rendered width). `box-sizing: border-box` is already correctly set, ruling that out as the cause. Left as a minor imperfection — the sidebar still fully reveals and is usable — rather than spending more debugging cycles on a 20px gap after the core "push" behavior was already confirmed working live.
- This is the only entry in the whole session verified via live DOM introspection end-to-end (computed grid columns, element rects, display values, before and after each fix) rather than static code reading — Chrome was used exclusively as an internal tool for this, never presented to the user as a preview, per their standing instruction to preview only in Firefox. Opened the final result in Firefox for the user's own check.


## LIME-11-fix5 — Reply avatars, reaction position, main-thread reply indicator

- **Item 1 ("reply avatars render as plain text") checked directly against the current code — still correct, third time this exact claim has come up (also in LIME-11-fix2, also already correct then).** `replyHtml()` already used a real `seed-avatar` span with `paintAvatar()` called on it; verified live via Chrome (internal diagnostic only, never shown as a preview): the rendered avatar computes `border-radius: 50%`, a real background color, and "JC" initials text — a genuine colored circle, not plain text. No code change made for this specific claim.
- Did find two real, concrete CSS issues in the same area though, and fixed both: `.lime-reply` was missing `align-items` entirely, defaulting to flex's own `stretch` — which would force the fixed-size avatar to stretch to match the content column's height instead of sitting fixed-size at the top (more noticeable the longer a reply's text or reaction row gets). Added `align-items: flex-start`. Separately, `.lime-message__reactions`'s shared rule (main thread) adds a 12px left-margin that makes sense there (aligning under the bubble's own text inset) but has no equivalent to align under in `.lime-reply`'s flatter layout — zeroed it out scoped specifically to `.lime-reply .lime-message__reactions`, leaving the main thread and quote (explicitly out of scope) untouched.
- New "X replies" indicator (item 4) — like reply counts and reactions elsewhere, computed live via `getRepliesForMessage(messageId).length`, never trusting the seed message's own stale `reply_count`. Click reuses the exact same `openReplies()` the "Reply" action button already calls — added as a second branch in that same delegated click listener, checking `.lime-message__reply-indicator` directly by its own `data-message-id` rather than needing a `.closest('.lime-message')` lookup.
- Verified via live Chrome introspection (internal only): the indicator shows "3 replies" on the one message with real seed replies, clicking it opens the panel to the correct quote (`msg-003`), the reply avatar computes as a real circle with real initials, and the reactions margin-left override reads `0px`. Opened the final result in Firefox for the user's own visual check.


## LIME-10-fix15 — Precise fade/padding calibration, verified live

- LIME-10-fix13's `padding-bottom: 150px` was an explicit reasoned estimate (Chrome wasn't available for previews at the time). Measured for real this time: `.lime-composer` is 107px tall collapsed, 163px with the toolbar expanded. Calibrated `padding-bottom` (200px) against the taller *expanded* height, not just the resting height — the JS auto-grow handler already rescrolls on every keystroke (LIME-10-fix13), but calibrating for the max height means there's never even a one-frame risk of clipping between the composer growing and that rescroll firing.
- Items 3 (`.lime-composer` bottom padding) and 4 (`.lime-composer__actions` top padding) were already exactly what this brief asked for — checked directly before touching anything. Item 4 in particular: the brief described "reducing" padding-top to 4px, but it was already `0` (tighter than requested) since LIME-10-fix13; no change made.
- Item 5 ("fade only visible when scrolling older messages up") reads as a description of the fade's *intended effect* once padding is correctly calibrated, not a literal request to scroll-gate it — the composer's fade has been deliberately non-scroll-gated since it was built (documented in `gradients.css`: "the composer is a permanent bar always sitting over the thread, not an overflow indicator"), and reversing that would be a real design change this brief didn't clearly ask for. Left the gating behavior alone; with clearance now positive in both composer states, the fade only ever visually masks content that's actually scrolled up into its ~40px zone, which is what item 5 describes.
- **Hit and fixed a real tooling snag while measuring**: `osascript ... set bounds of front window` repeatedly failed to actually resize Chrome (window stuck at ~500px width, confirmed via `window.innerWidth`), which meant `.lime-conversation` legitimately rendered `display:none` (correct "contacts" mobile-view behavior at that width) and every composer measurement came back zero — not a bug in this app's own CSS, a test-harness limitation. Worked around it by forcing `data-mobile-view="thread"` directly via a live DOM mutation in the diagnostic script itself, rather than fighting the window resize further.
- Verified live via Chrome (internal diagnostic only, never shown as a preview): clearance between the last message and the composer's top edge is +93px collapsed and +37px expanded — both positive, confirming no clipping in either state. Opened the final result in Firefox for the user's own visual check of the fade appearance itself, which no measurement can substitute for.


## LIME-12-fix3 — Real escaping-content bug, found via elementFromPoint

- A second brief also labeled "LIME-12-fix2" arrived just before this one asking to rebuild the mobile nav as an overlay-with-dark-backdrop — directly reversing what the user explicitly asked for two briefs earlier (push model, no dark tint). Flagged the contradiction and asked before touching anything; user confirmed keeping the push model, so that brief was treated as stale/superseded with no code change.
- **This brief's own diagnosis (position:fixed/absolute on `.lime-nav`/`.lime-sidebar`) was wrong — checked directly, none of those classes have any such rule — but the underlying symptom (nav content escaping the hidden container) was real and reproducible.** Caught a real flaw in my own first verification method along the way: `getBoundingClientRect()` reports a descendant's nominal layout position regardless of whether an ancestor's `overflow: hidden` is actually clipping it from view — it doesn't account for clipping at all. Switched to `document.elementFromPoint()`, which asks what's actually rendered at a given pixel, and confirmed the sidebar's logo `<img>` and `.lime-sidebar__top` genuinely were visible/clickable at coordinates within the supposedly-0px-wide collapsed panel.
- First fix attempt (explicit `padding: 0`) was based on a wrong theory (Seed's unconditional 16px padding fighting `overflow:hidden`) and had zero measured effect — this file already zeroes that padding unconditionally elsewhere (confirmed via grep), so padding was never the actual cause.
- **Real root cause, confirmed via `getComputedStyle()` rather than assumed**: `.seed-layout__left`'s `overflow: hidden` was computing to `visible` despite the rule declaring `hidden` plainly — something else (never fully identified with certainty; most likely Seed's own `overflow-x`/`overflow-y` longhand split interacting with this shorthand declaration in a way plain specificity/source-order reasoning didn't predict) kept winning. Adding `!important` was the one change that actually flipped the computed value to `hidden` and made `elementFromPoint()` stop returning sidebar content. Added explicit `width: 0; max-width: 0;` too, a harder constraint than relying on the grid track's inherited sizing alone, and restored real values (`280px`, `var(--seed-space-4)` padding) for the open state.
- Verified via live Chrome (internal diagnostic only, never shown as a preview): 3 probe points across the collapsed panel all resolve to `#layout` (nothing from the sidebar), `overflow` computes to `hidden`, and — to make sure the fix didn't accidentally break the *open* state — probing at the actual coordinates of the revealed search button correctly resolves to its icon, confirming the sidebar is still fully functional once opened. Opened the final result in Firefox for the user's own visual check.


## LIME-12-fix4 — Toolbar overflow menu, shorter placeholder

- **Item 1 (contained overlay drawer) skipped, not implemented** — it's a third variant of the same overlay-with-`position:fixed` pattern the user has now explicitly rejected twice this session (once in conversation, once via an earlier conflicting "LIME-12-fix2" brief they confirmed staying with the push model against). Its underlying concern (nav content floating/escaping) was also already fixed for real in LIME-12-fix3 (the `overflow: visible` bug), so there was nothing left to address here that wouldn't mean reversing a settled decision.
- Item 2's literal CSS ask (`font-size: 14px` on the placeholder at ≤480px) checked out as a no-op before touching anything — the placeholder already inherits `--seed-text-sm`, which *is* 14px, at every width. The real mechanism behind "placeholder overflows" is more likely that a `<textarea>` placeholder has no native ellipsis truncation the way a single-line `<input>`'s does, so it just wraps onto a second line in a narrow viewport, which the fixed single-line collapsed height then crudely clips. Implemented the brief's own offered alternative instead — shorter placeholder text via JS, gated to `innerWidth <= 480` — since it's the one of the two options that actually changes anything.
- Toolbar overflow menu (items 3/4) applied to *both* the main and reply composer toolbars for consistency, even though this brief's scope didn't explicitly exclude the main-only edits some earlier briefs (e.g. LIME-11-fix2) did — leaving the reply composer's identical toolbar without the same overflow treatment would have meant it just breaks differently on narrow screens instead. `dew-more-horizontal` doesn't exist (checked); used `dew-ellipsis-menu`, already the real icon for every other "More" button in this app. The overflow dropdown just relists the same option names as plain decorative rows — none of the toolbar's tools have any real formatting behavior wired at any width, so there's nothing functional to preserve by moving or cloning the actual buttons into it.
- Verified live via Chrome (internal diagnostic only, never shown as a preview): the overflow button and dropdown exist, open correctly, and list the right 5 items. Could not force the actual window below the 480px breakpoint in this sandbox (the same window-resize unreliability noted in LIME-10-fix15/LIME-12-fix3), so the media query's *trigger* itself is unverified live — confirmed correct by direct source reading instead, a lower-risk substitute for a standard, previously-proven media-query pattern. A styleSheet-based rule-existence check also attempted, but failed for an already-known reason (LIME-10-fix5's file:// `cssRules` access restriction) rather than indicating an actual problem — noted so it isn't mistaken for a real finding later.


## LIME-12-fix5 — Mobile drawer showed collapsed icon-only rail, not the full sidebar

- **Root cause confirmed via live introspection before touching any CSS.** `#layout` can carry a stale `seed-layout--collapsed-left` class left over from an earlier desktop session (persisted via `localStorage`, same leftover already dealt with once for `min-width` in LIME-12-fix2). That class's ~10 descendant rules exist to make the *desktop* icon-only collapse work — hiding `.lime-nav__label`, `.lime-nav-search__label`, `[data-brand-wordmark]`, `.lime-sidebar__user-name`, `.lime-badge--soon`, and centering `.lime-nav__btn`/`.lime-nav-search`/`.lime-sidebar__user-trigger` into a 40px rail — but were never scoped to desktop-only. LIME-12-fix2 only fixed the panel's own `min-width`; it left every one of these descendant rules still applying once the drawer opened, so the panel rendered at the correct 280px push-width but with all its labels hidden and icons centered as if still collapsed. Reproduced live: with `seed-layout--collapsed-left` forced onto `#layout` and the drawer opened, `getComputedStyle()` on the label/wordmark/username elements all returned `display: none` — matching the reported bug exactly.
- Fixed by adding one override block scoped to `.seed-layout--mobile-open.seed-layout--collapsed-left .X`, restoring each affected selector back to its real expanded/base value (read directly from the base rules first, not guessed) — labels back to their default `inline`, `[data-brand-wordmark]`/`.lime-sidebar__user-name` back to `block`, `.lime-nav__btn`/`.lime-nav-search`/`.lime-sidebar__user-trigger` back to full-width/`justify-content: flex-start` with their real padding, `.lime-sidebar__top`/`.lime-sidebar__brand`/`.lime-nav` back to their real flex/padding values. `!important` throughout, matching the specificity-fight lesson already documented in LIME-12-fix3 for this exact class-interaction area — plain declarations lost to the collapsed-left rules in testing here too. Left `.seed-layout--collapsed-left .lime-sidebar__toggle`'s hover-reveal rule alone: `#left-panel-toggle` (the element it targets) is already unconditionally `display: none` at this breakpoint, so it can never actually apply on mobile regardless of the collapsed-left class.
- Verified live via Chrome (internal diagnostic only, never shown as a preview — forced the stale class, opened the drawer, read computed styles): `navLabelDisplay`, `wordmarkDisplay`, `userNameDisplay`, `searchLabelDisplay`, and `badgeDisplay` all now compute `block` (previously `none`), and the panel still measures 312px wide (280px column + the drawer's own padding, unchanged from fix2/fix3 — confirms the push-model width wasn't disturbed by this fix). The temporary diagnostic script used for this and the two prior fixes has been fully removed from `public/index.html`, restoring it to a clean diff (verified: `git diff public/index.html` empty). Opened the final result in Firefox for the user's own visual check.

No overlay/backdrop code was touched.

Narrowed the mobile-open grid column + panel from 280px to 240px (`.seed-layout--mobile-open .seed-layout__left` and the matching `.seed-layout.seed-layout--mobile-open` grid-template-columns, both in `lime.css`'s mobile media queries).

Desktop's real convention (confirmed by re-reading `.seed-layout__left`'s base rule) is the opposite: the panel container itself is flush (0 padding), and each row manages its own inset independently (`.lime-sidebar__top`, `.lime-nav`, etc.). Removed the outer panel's padding entirely to match that convention, and updated `.lime-sidebar__top`'s own mobile-open padding-left from desktop's 24px (sized to optically center the logo over nav icons in a permanent rail) down to 12px — the same inset `.lime-center-top`'s hamburger button and `.lime-nav`'s own row already use.

With it open, the logo mark's left edge now also measures x=28 — exact match, confirmed in both the normal case and the stale-`collapsed-left`-class edge case fixed in LIME-12-fix5 (re-tested via a `?collapsedLeft=1` query param to force it deterministically, since the real stale-class trigger depends on desktop history this session had already left in `localStorage`). Panel width measures exactly 240px in both cases too, and `box-sizing: border-box` on the panel keeps that number an accurate rendered width now instead of the 280px-plus-padding overshoot LIME-12-fix5 measured. The temporary diagnostic script (added and removed for this verification) leaves `public/index.html` with an empty diff against HEAD — confirmed via `git diff public/index.html`. ## LIME-13 — QA pass on hand-made sidebar/composer edits

- The user's own uncommitted hand-made edits (unchanged, committed as design intent): `.lime-nav-search` dropped its 8px bottom margin (the 24px row gap already spaces it); `.lime-sidebar__footer`/`.lime-sidebar__user-trigger` rebuilt to mirror `.lime-nav__btn`'s pattern (40px-tall button, 12px gap, transparent background, `--lime-nav-hover` on hover, own `border-radius`/font tokens instead of the old plain-div-with-padding style); the Send button's active and active-hover states pointed at `--lime-nav-hover` instead of the old calm-bg tokens; `textarea.lime-composer__input` got an explicit 8px left inset via new `padding-left`/`padding-right` longhands after the shorthand.
- **Fix 1 (real bug):** the new `padding-right: var(--seed-space-2)` longhand overrode the shorthand's 40px right padding, which exists solely to reserve room for the absolutely-positioned Send button — typed text would render under it on a long single line. Folded both longhands back into the shorthand: `padding: var(--seed-space-1) 40px var(--seed-space-1) var(--seed-space-2)`, keeping the user's 8px left inset and restoring the 40px right.

Gave `.seed-layout--collapsed-left .lime-sidebar__footer` the matching `var(--seed-space-2)` (8px) padding, and set the collapsed trigger to `width: 40px; padding: 0; justify-content: center;` — same scheme as `.lime-nav__btn`'s collapsed override, so the avatar (24px, centered) is no longer squeezed into a 16px content box.
- **Fix 3 (mobile drawer stale-class override):** the LIME-12-fix5 override restoring the trigger inside a stale `collapsed-left` mobile drawer still targeted the *old* padding scheme and didn't account for Fix 2's new rail width/footer padding. Added a matching `.seed-layout--mobile-open.seed-layout--collapsed-left .lime-sidebar__footer` override (8px/12px, same as the equivalent `.lime-nav` override two rules up) and updated the trigger override to `width: 100% !important; padding: 0 var(--seed-space-3) !important;` — same values as `.lime-nav__btn`'s own mobile-open collapsed-left override, so both rows align.
- **Fix 4:** replaced the stale 3-line comment on `.lime-composer__return.is-active` (still explaining a `--calm-bg-subtle-default` choice that Fix 1's predecessor edit had already replaced with `--lime-nav-hover`) with a comment describing the actual current rationale. Left `.lime-composer__return.is-active:hover` in place, unchanged — still needed to outrank the later `.lime-composer__return:hover` rule at equal specificity.
- **Verification note:** Claude in Chrome was not connected this session, so the six checks in the brief's Phase 3 were done by computed-value reasoning against the file rather than live pixel measurement (no live-layout ambiguity in any of the four fixes — all are static padding/width arithmetic, cross-checked against the nav's own existing, already-shipped collapsed/mobile rules for the matching numbers). Confirmed: `textarea.lime-composer__input`'s shared rule (no separate `#replies-composer` override exists) yields `padding-right: 40px; padding-left: 8px` for both composers. `.lime-sidebar__footer`/`.lime-nav` use identical 12px left insets on desktop (matching left edges) and identical 8px insets in the collapsed rail (identical 40×40 trigger/button sizing, avatar no longer clipped). The mobile-open collapsed-left override for the footer/trigger now numerically matches `.lime-nav`/`.lime-nav__btn`'s own mobile-open collapsed-left override line for line. The mobile drawer without a stale `collapsed-left` class was already correct pre-fix (no separate override exists there; base rules already give full width and visible name) — unaffected by these fixes. Send background states unchanged by this brief (`--lime-nav-hover` on active/active-hover, `--calm-bg-subtle-hover` on plain hover) — only the comment above them changed.

- Added the missing hover/pressed steps the brief called for, in the source order it specified so pressed always wins ties: `.lime-nav__btn--active:hover` → hover step, `.lime-nav__btn:active` → active step (placed after both `:hover` rules, same specificity, so source order resolves the tie toward the deeper color while pressed); `.lime-sidebar__user-trigger:active` and `.lime-composer__return.is-active:active` likewise.

## LIME-15 — Keep the drawer's logo mark visible on hover at mobile widths

- Root cause matched the brief exactly: the desktop collapsed-rail hover swap (`.seed-layout--collapsed-left .lime-sidebar__brand:hover [data-brand-mark] { opacity: 0 }`, line 393) fades the mark out expecting `.lime-sidebar__toggle` to fade in over it — but that toggle is `display: none` at ≤767px (line 1179, the hamburger lives in the center panel instead). If `#layout` still carries a stale `seed-layout--collapsed-left` class from a prior desktop session (persisted via `localStorage`), hovering the brand row on mobile faded the mark to nothing, with no replacement visible — an empty gap before the wordmark.
- Fix: added one `!important` override of the same selector, scoped inside the existing `@media (max-width: 767px)` block (directly after the `[data-brand-wordmark]` override at line 3061), forcing `opacity: 1` regardless of the stale class. Desktop's own hover-swap rule (outside the media query) is untouched — it still swaps mark for toggle when the rail is genuinely collapsed on desktop.
- **Verification:** confirmed via source reading (Chrome not connected this session) that the fix rule sits inside the correct media query and its `!important` beats the desktop rule's plain declaration at ≤767px, while desktop-width behavior is unaffected since the media query simply doesn't apply there. Brace count balanced (420/420). `git diff --stat` shows only `public/css/lime.css`.
- Opened `file:///Users/shem/Sites/lime/public/index.html` in Firefox for the user's check: narrow window with the menu open (hovering the logo should keep the mark visible) and wide window with the sidebar collapsed (hovering should still swap to the toggle, unchanged).
- **Gate feedback (2026-09-27):** user's check surfaced two issues via screenshot. (1) Widening the window while the drawer is open leaves a grey overlap until clicked — the known leftover backdrop from the old overlay design; already drafted as LIME-16, next in queue, not fixed here. (2) The drawer's logo mark didn't align with the nav icons below it — not in either drafted brief, addressed as a same-day follow-up below.

No other structural change — `.lime-sidebar__brand`'s own rules (flex/justify-content) were untouched, and the hover-swap behavior stays exactly as LIME-15 left it (mark always visible on mobile, no toggle to swap to).

## LIME-16 — Removed the leftover push-model backdrop, closer stays in sync

- Root cause matched the brief exactly: `#mobile-nav-backdrop` (`index.html` line 38) was a leftover from the pre-push-model overlay design (LIME-12-fix2 replaced the overlay with a push, but never removed the backdrop element or its wiring). `applyOpen()` in the mobile nav IIFE still toggled `.is-open` on it every time the drawer opened. A media-query rule force-hid it with `display: none !important` at ≤767px only — widen the window past that breakpoint while the drawer is open (the drawer itself has no width-based auto-close) and that hiding rule stops applying, so `.lime-mobile-nav-backdrop.is-open { display: block }` wins and the dark 30%-opacity tint covers the whole app. This is exactly what the user's screenshot showed.
- **Latent bug fixed through the same mechanism, per the brief:** `wireHoverPreviewToggle` (shared with the left-panel toggle) keeps its own private `state` var. The mobile IIFE's Escape handler called `applyOpen(false)` directly, bypassing that state — so after Escape, the next hamburger click would toggle state back to `true → false` (already closed, no-op) instead of opening. Traced the logic by hand (state stays `true` after Escape, next click flips it to `false`, `apply(false)` on an already-closed drawer does nothing) — reproduces the "have to click twice" complaint without needing to click through it live.
- Fix: `wireHoverPreviewToggle` now returns `{ set(next) }`, letting an external closer (Escape, or a breakpoint change) update its private state and repaint/apply in sync — the left-panel toggle's own call site (line 603) ignores the return value, unaffected, since it never needed external closing.
- Mobile nav IIFE: removed every backdrop reference (the `getElementById`, the null-guard, the `classList.toggle`, the click listener). Escape now calls `nav.set(false)`. Added a `matchMedia('(max-width: 767px)')` change listener that calls `nav.set(false)` when the query stops matching (i.e., the window widens past mobile) — this is what actually fixes the overlap, since the drawer now closes itself instead of relying on a backdrop or CSS alone to hide symptoms.
- Removed `#mobile-nav-backdrop` from `index.html`, `.lime-mobile-nav-backdrop`/`.lime-mobile-nav-backdrop.is-open` from `lime.css`, and the now-dead `display: none !important` override that was inside the mobile media query (its own job — hiding the backdrop at mobile widths — is moot with the backdrop gone).
- Left the 768px-vs-767px breakpoint mismatch alone, per the brief — used `767px` in the new `matchMedia` call to match this codebase's existing mobile queries exactly. Still logged as its own open item in `PLOT.md`, unrelated to this fix.
- **Verification:** `grep -rn 'mobile-nav-backdrop' public/` returns nothing. Brace count balanced (417/417). `node --check public/js/app.js` passes. `git diff --stat` touches exactly `app.js`, `index.html`, `lime.css` (no `TEND.md` change tracked in the count until this entry). Chrome wasn't connected this session, so the four gate scenarios were traced through the code path by hand rather than clicked through live: (a) Escape then one hamburger click reopens, since `nav.set(false)` keeps the toggle's internal state accurate; (b) widening past 767px fires the `matchMedia` listener, which removes `seed-layout--mobile-open` and — since the backdrop no longer exists at all — there's nothing left to render a tint; (c) narrowing back leaves it closed with correct state, so one click reopens; (d) the search modal's own backdrop (`search-modal-backdrop`, lines 942–993) was untouched entirely, a separate, correct mechanism.
- Opened `file:///Users/shem/Sites/lime/public/index.html` in Firefox for the user's check: narrow → open the menu → widen — no dark overlay. Open the menu, press Escape, click the hamburger once — it should open on the first click. Confirm the search modal still opens normally.
- **Note:** PLOT.md's continuation note had said LIME-16 had no commit yet — stale by the time it was read; this session's own prior turn had already landed it as `77bb100`.


## LIME-17 — Live sync between the thread panel and the main thread

- Confirmed the survey's premise exactly: conv-001 has 9 messages, 3 of them (`msg-004`, `msg-1004`, `msg-1005`) are replies to `msg-003` via `reply_to`, and `renderThread` was rendering all 9 inline since `getMessagesByConversation` doesn't exclude replies. Verified with a small node script evaluating `data.js` directly against the real seed data (not guessed).
- **`data.js`:** added `getThreadMessages(conversationId)` — `getMessagesByConversation` filtered to messages without `reply_to`. `renderThread` now calls this instead, so replies never render as ordinary main-thread messages. Confirmed via the same node script: the returned set for conv-001 is exactly the other 6 messages, and `getRepliesForMessage('msg-003')` still correctly returns 3.
- **Reaction sync:** added `renderReactionsEverywhere(messageId, reactions)`, placed inside the same IIFE as `REACTABLE` (not top-level, since it reads that IIFE-scoped constant) right after its declaration. Both reaction branches (the picker's emoji click and the pill's toggle click) now call it instead of `renderReactions` on just the clicked container — it re-renders every element matching `REACTABLE` (`.lime-message`, `.lime-reply`, `.lime-replies-panel__quote`) that shares the same `data-message-id`, so a reaction added in the thread panel's quote shows up on the same message in the main thread and vice versa.
- **Reply indicator refresh:** moved `replyIndicatorHtml` out of the contacts/thread IIFE to top level, next to `reactionsHtml`, unchanged. Added a new top-level `refreshReplyIndicator(messageId)`: finds the message in `#thread-messages` by `data-message-id`, removes any existing `.lime-message__reply-indicator`, and inserts a freshly computed one directly after `.lime-message__reactions` — works whether the message previously had zero replies (no existing indicator to remove) or already had some. Called from `submitReply` immediately after `sendReply(...)`, so "N replies" updates in the main thread the instant a reply is sent, without waiting for a full re-render.
- **Verification:** `node --check` passes on both `app.js` and `data.js`. `git diff --stat` touches only `app.js` and `data.js`, as scoped. Chrome wasn't connected this session, so the reaction-sync and reply-refresh behavior was verified by tracing the code paths rather than clicking through live: `renderQuote` sets the quote element's `data-message-id` to the real parent message's id, so it's a genuine `REACTABLE` match for the same id as the main-thread bubble; `refreshReplyIndicator`'s anchor (`.lime-message__reactions`, always present in `messageHtml`'s output) means the insert works regardless of whether an indicator previously existed. Switching conversations and back re-runs `renderThread` from scratch via `getThreadMessages`, so replies stay excluded — confirmed by reading `messageHtml`, which still calls `replyIndicatorHtml` per rendered message.
- Opened `file:///Users/shem/Sites/lime/public/index.html` in Firefox for the user's check: react on the quoted message in the thread panel and confirm the same reaction shows on the original message in the main thread (and the reverse); send a reply and confirm "N replies" updates immediately; confirm replies never appear as their own message bubbles in the main thread.
- **Gate feedback (2026-09-27):** the reaction/reply sync itself checked out (the one message shown in both places had matching reactions). Two things the user flagged turned out to be already-scoped-elsewhere: no replier avatars and no "last reply" time on the main-thread indicator is exactly what LIME-18 (next) restores — LIME-17 deliberately kept the plain indicator. But a third thing was a real, previously-unreported bug: the reaction picker on the reply panel's first reply appeared as a stray floating emoji instead of the full picker. Addressed below, same day, before moving to LIME-18.


## LIME-17-fix — Reaction picker no longer clipped on the reply panel's first row

- **Root cause, confirmed by reading the CSS (Chrome not connected this session):** `.lime-replies-panel__list` scrolls (`overflow-y: auto`), and `.lime-reaction-picker` opened `position: absolute; top: -48px` relative to its row. For the *first* reply in the list, that pushes the picker above the scroll container's own top edge — with nothing further up to scroll into, the browser has no choice but to clip it, leaving only whatever sliver of the 5-emoji row happened to poke through visible (what looked like a single stray 🙂 in the user's screenshot). This is the exact same class of bug LIME-03w already fixed once for the nav dropdowns being clipped by `.seed-layout__left`'s `overflow-x: hidden` — pre-existing since `.lime-reply` first got its own picker (LIME-11-fix2), not something LIME-17 introduced, just newly exercised by testing reactions in the panel.
- Applied the same fix LIME-03w established: `.lime-reaction-picker` is now `position: fixed` (was `absolute`), with no baked-in `top`/`right` — those are computed once, in JS, from the row's own `getBoundingClientRect()` at the moment the React button opens it (`picker.style.right`/`picker.style.bottom`, mirroring `wireDropdownToggle`'s existing `fixed: true` mode). `position: fixed` escapes the scrolling ancestor's clipping entirely, the same way it already does for the notification/user/voice-mode dropdowns living in the equally-scrollable `.seed-layout__left`.
- Scoped to the shared `.lime-reaction-picker`/React-button click handler, so the fix applies uniformly wherever a picker can open (main thread, reply panel, quote) rather than a `.lime-reply`-only patch — the same clipping risk exists in principle for the very first message in the main thread if scrolled to the top, though that wasn't reported and is far less likely to be hit in practice (the thread auto-scrolls to bottom on load/send). One mechanism, no new landmine left behind for a sibling context.
- Position is only recomputed when the picker transitions from closed to open (`!picker.classList.contains('is-open')`), matching `wireDropdownToggle`'s existing pattern — closing doesn't need it, and a stale position from a prior open is always overwritten before the next.
- **Verification:** `node --check public/js/app.js` passes. Brace count in `lime.css` balanced (417/417). `git diff --stat` touches only `app.js` and `lime.css`. Confirmed by reading the DOM structure that `.lime-replies-panel` sits inside `#right-panel`/`.seed-layout__right`, the same kind of ancestor chain the already-proven nav-dropdown fix escapes from — no `transform` on any resting-state ancestor that would defeat `position: fixed`'s containing-block escape.
- Opened `file:///Users/shem/Sites/lime/public/index.html` in Firefox for the user's check: open the reply panel on a message with replies, hover the very first reply, click React — the full 5-emoji picker should appear fully visible (not clipped), and picking one should react normally.
- **Gate confirmed (2026-09-27):** user confirmed both LIME-17's sync and this fix work.


## LIME-18 — Restored the reply summary (avatars, count, last reply) on thread messages

- **Survey correction:** the brief's background cited commit `fe4631b` as the source of the original avatars-included design. Checked directly (`git show fe4631b:public/index.html`) — `fe4631b`'s own file state already has *no* avatars, just the plain `4 replies<span class="lime-replies__time">Last reply 7 days ago</span>` button; the avatars existed in `fe4631b`'s *parent* commit, and `fe4631b` itself is what removed them while introducing the nested-time-span phrasing. Doesn't change anything — Phase 2 gave an exact target markup that fuses both historical versions, which is what got built — just correcting the record.

Verified against the exact shipped source (extracted the real function text, not retyped, and eval'd it against fabricated dates): now/earlier-today → `today at 2:09 AM`; yesterday → `yesterday at 2:09 AM`; 3 days → `3 days ago`; 40 days → `Aug 18`. All five match the brief's spec exactly.

Avatars are the distinct reply senders, most-recent-first, capped at 5 (deduped via a `Set` while walking replies in reverse). Given `.seed-avatar-group`'s `row-reverse` layout, this means the array's first (most-recent) entry ends up *behind and to the right*, not frontmost — flagged in a comment and here, per the survey step's own instruction not to touch that CSS, just report the outcome.
- **`refreshReplyIndicator`** now replaces the whole `.lime-message__footer` (avatars + count + time all change together, not just a count) and explicitly repaints the newly inserted avatars via `paintAvatar` — they're inserted after the page's one-time load-time avatar-paint pass has already run, same root cause LIME-11 already fixed once for `handleSend`'s own sent-message avatar.

Same root cause class as the `handleSend`/`refreshReplyIndicator` cases above, now covered by one added line.

Left the four restored rules (`.lime-message__footer` etc.) completely untouched, including their "underlined, dark text" look the brief flagged as a possible future tuning item — not touched here, per scope.

Behavioral checks run against the exact shipped source (extracted by line range, not retyped) plus the real seed data: `msg-003`'s indicator renders "3 replies" with avatars "Jean Chung" then "Shem Robinson" (Jean is `msg-1005`'s sender, the most recent of the 3 real replies); simulating a new reply from the current user bumps it to "4 replies" with "Shem Robinson" now first and "Last reply today at {now}" — matching the brief's own check 3 literally (count and DOM-order-first correct; the *visual* frontmost question is the flagged `seed-avatar-group` quirk above, unverifiable without Chrome this session).
