Rewrote the four original policies on these helpers and added the eleven the brief listed as missing: `conversation_members` select (for members) and insert (creator/owner adds a member); `conversations` insert (creator); `messages` select/insert for communities and update/delete of your own; `message_reactions` select/insert/delete. **15 policies total, one line each:**
   - `conversations_select_member`, `conversations_select_community`, `conversations_insert_creator`, `conversations_update_owner`
   - `conversation_members_select_member`, `conversation_members_insert_creator`, `conversation_members_update_self`
   - `messages_select_member`, `messages_select_community`, `messages_insert_member`, `messages_update_self`, `messages_delete_self`
   - `message_reactions_select_member`, `message_reactions_insert_self`, `message_reactions_delete_self`
   - Every one of the four RLS-enabled tables has its own `select` policy defined, confirmed by re-reading the list above against the table list rather than just asserting it.
   - **One thing caught and self-corrected before it shipped, not asked about:** first pass also added `alter table profiles enable row level security;` with no matching policy — the brief's five issues never asked for `profiles` RLS at all, and enabling it with zero policies would have made every profile completely unreadable by anyone. Removed before finishing, since it was my own addition, not something to leave for a stop-and-ask.
   - `message_reactions_insert_self` and `message_reactions_select_member` both check `is_member` of the reacting message's conversation, not just row ownership — the brief's own phrase was "insert/delete of your own rows only," but insert-only-your-own-rows without also checking membership would let a non-member insert a reaction on a message they can't even select. Flagging this as a small, deliberate addition beyond the letter of the brief, not a deviation from its intent.
3. **DM uniqueness.** Added `conversations.dm_key text` (the two member ids, sorted, joined with `:`, null for non-direct types) and `create unique index ... on conversations (dm_key) where dm_key is not null`. Documented in both `schema.sql`'s column comment and `data-model.md`'s mapping table that the *store* computes `dm_key`, never the UI.
4. **Status mapping documented.** Added a mapping-table row: `presenceFor(profiles.status)` maps `online/busy/offline` → `Active/Busy/Away`; "Away" and "DND" as UI-visible states have no distinct stored value today (confirmed by re-reading `PRESENCE`/`PRESENCE_LABEL` in `app.js` directly — `dnd` isn't reachable from any real `presenceFor` output at all, it only exists in the still-static Recent row's hardcoded markup).
5. **Reactor rule replaced.** Removed the earlier "dedupe the senders" suggestion; the decided rule is now the conversation's distinct members (participant order), sliced to `min(count, member_count)` — reasoning: a reaction count can't exceed how many people could possibly react, which deduping alone doesn't enforce. Updated both `schema.sql`'s comment and `data-model.md`'s known-gaps section (moved from "open" to "resolved," kept for the record rather than deleted) with the same `conv-001`/`msg-006` example, now showing 😍×5 → 😍×2 (a 2-person DM). Noted that `seed-data/seed.ts` itself is changed to this rule in LIME-24b, not here.

**Verification:** re-read both docs end to end for internal consistency after all five changes (not just the diffs in isolation) — no remaining direct `auth.uid()` comparisons outside `current_profile_id()`'s own body (confirmed by `grep`), every cross-reference between the two files (auth seam ↔ switch checklist ↔ schema.sql comments) points at something that still exists after the edits. No SQL execution available in this environment (same as LIME-24a — no `psql`, no local Postgres, confirmed by checking), so verified by careful reading rather than running it.

**Gate:** plot re-reviews. Per the brief, the user needs nothing here beyond the reaction-count note (😍×5 → 😍×2 in Jean's DM, which becomes visible once LIME-24b lands) — already carried forward from LIME-24a's own gate note, now with the corrected number.


## LIME-24b — Store + local adapter on the data contract, persisted, session-based current user

**Plot's one-line amendment, applied first:** `docs/schema.sql`'s `messages_insert_member` policy checked membership but not the sender, so a member could insert a message as someone else. Now `with check (is_member(conversation_id) and sender_id = current_profile_id())`.

**Phase 1 survey:** ~35 real call sites in `app.js` touching a `data.js` function or `CURRENT_USER_ID` (`getTeacherById` ×7, `getConversationTitle`/`getLatestActivity` ×4 each, `otherParticipants` ×3, `CURRENT_USER_ID` ×4, and single-digit counts for the rest) — under the brief's own ~40-site split threshold, so this landed as one commit, not a reads/writes split.

**Architecture:** `data.js` retired entirely (deleted) and replaced by two new files, loaded in order (`seed-data.js` → `local-adapter.js` → `store.js` → `app.js`):
- **`local-adapter.js` (`LocalAdapter`):** normalizes the embedded seed into the five contract tables on first load; persists/loads a `lime-state-v1` snapshot guarded by a djb2 fingerprint of the current embedded seed (a stale snapshot from before a seed change is discarded, not silently trusted); `reset()` just removes the snapshot key.
- **`store.js` (`LimeStore`):** the full contract from `docs/data-model.md` — every read listed there, every write (each returns a Promise, updates the in-memory cache synchronously, persists through the adapter debounced ~100ms, then emits its documented event), `init()`/`reset()`. `LIME_BACKEND = 'local'` exists as the seam the switch checklist describes, with only the local branch actually implemented — no speculative `SupabaseAdapter` stub built ahead of need.
- Both files wrap their internals in an IIFE and expose only `LocalAdapter`/`LimeStore` — classic `<script>` tags already share one lexical scope (confirmed live: `app.js`'s own `LimeStore.xxx()` calls work via that, not via `window`), but a `const` at a script's top level isn't automatically a `window` property, so `store.js` also does `window.LimeStore = LimeStore;` explicitly at the end — for anything reaching in from outside the page's own scripts (this session's own test harness needed exactly this; devtools would too), not because the app itself needed it.

**One real mistake in this brief's own draft, caught before ever running it, not shipped:** writing the corrected reactor rule into `seed-data/seed.ts`, I first referenced a variable named `conv` assuming it was the current conversation — it wasn't; that name belongs to an *earlier, already-finished* `for` loop in the same function (conversations are seeded before messages, in two separate loops, not one nested loop). Caught by re-reading the surrounding function structure before treating the edit as done, fixed to look the conversation up by `msg.conversation_id` directly. Mentioning it because the lesson generalizes: a plausible variable name in a large function is not the same as confirming what's actually in scope at that point.

**The change, matching the brief's 9 Phase 2 items:**
1. **Normalize at load:** `profiles`/`conversations` (with `dm_key` for DMs)/`conversation_members` (from `participants`, owner = `created_by`)/`messages`/`message_reactions` built from the embedded seed. **Reactions use the corrected LIME-24a-fix rule** (conversation's distinct members, participant order, sliced to `min(count, member_count)`) — applied to **both** `LocalAdapter` and `seed-data/seed.ts` (the only change allowed there), so a local seed and a database seed agree, per the brief.
2. **The store** implements every read/write/event/lifecycle method from `docs/data-model.md`, described above.
3. **Auth seam:** `getCurrentUserId()` reads `lime-demo-session`'s `email`, matches it against cached `profiles.email`, falls back to `teacher-002` with a once-only `console.log` on no match. `CURRENT_USER_ID` is gone from `app.js` entirely — every one of its ~4 call sites now reads a `currentUserId` local (captured once per closure from `LimeStore.getCurrentUserId()`) or calls the store directly.
4. **Reactions:** `reactionsHtml`/`renderReactions` now take just a `messageId` and call `LimeStore.getReactions(messageId)` for `{emoji, count, mine}` directly — `mine` drives `.lime-reaction--active`. `userReactedKeys` is gone. **The old add-always/toggle-existing pair (`addReaction` vs `toggleReaction`) collapsed into the contract's single `toggleReaction`** — both the reaction picker (adding a new emoji) and an existing pill (removing one) now call the exact same store method; a single `lime:reactions-changed` subscription re-renders every on-screen instance of that message (main thread, reply quote, reply list) rather than each click handler re-rendering off its own result.
   - **Confirmed seed side effect, exactly as flagged:** `msg-006`'s 😍 reaction is now `{emoji: "😍", count: 2, mine: true}` — a 2-person DM can't have 5 reactors, and the current user is one of the 2.
5. **Async writes:** `handleSend` calls `LimeStore.sendMessage(id, { content }).then(...)` to render the sent message from the resolved record; clearing the composer stays synchronous/instant, not gated on the write. `submitReply` now calls `LimeStore.sendMessage(parent.conversation_id, { content, replyTo: parentId })` — **the old separate `sendReply`/`lime:activity` pair is gone**; the contract's `sendMessage` already covers replies via `replyTo`, and its own `lime:messages-changed` event is what the list re-sorts on, so no manual event dispatch is needed in `submitReply` any more. `LimeStore.init().then(initMessagesList)` wraps the one function that actually needs the store ready before it runs; nothing else in `app.js` needed wrapping (see the ordering note below).
6. **Delegated clicks:** the Recent-row highlight and the mobile view router's contact click are now `document.addEventListener('click', ...)` with `e.target.closest(...)`, replacing the two one-time `querySelectorAll().forEach()` bindings — rows built after page load (or, now, rebuilt/removed) are covered automatically.
7. **The list subscribes to `lime:conversations-changed` and `lime:messages-changed`.** Rewrote the update logic as one `refreshList()` (recomputes the full sorted membership from the store on every relevant event, diffs it against the DOM's existing rows — update-in-place for a row that still exists, build a fresh one for a conversation that's newly a member of, remove a row for one that's no longer in the list) rather than the old assume-nothing-added-or-removed reorder-only pass — this is a genuine behavior addition beyond a literal "keep working," but the brief's own phrasing ("rows can now be created at any time") implies the symmetric removal case too, and it cost nothing extra to implement given the loop's own shape. `lime:activity` is gone.
8. **Reset:** `#reset-demo-data-btn` (`dew-undo` icon) added between Settings and Sign Out in the profile menu; a native `confirm()`, then `LimeStore.reset().then(() => location.reload())`.
9. **`can(action, conversation)`:** implemented exactly per the doc (owner-only rename/delete, DMs never renameable). Confirmed nothing in today's UI calls it yet, per the brief.

**A real ordering issue found and fixed, not in the brief's own list:** deferring the main list's own render into `LimeStore.init().then(...)` means it now runs as a microtask *after* the rest of the synchronous script — including the pre-existing one-time `document.querySelectorAll('.lime-avatar[data-name]').forEach(paintAvatar)` sweep further down the file, which used to catch the list's own avatars because that render used to run synchronously *before* the sweep. Deferred, the sweep would fire first and simply never see rows that don't exist yet, silently leaving the whole list unpainted. Fixed by having the list's own `buildRow()` paint its own avatars directly, the same established pattern `selectConversation`/`handleSend`/`refreshReplyIndicator` already use for exactly this class of problem (LIME-11, LIME-18). Confirmed live (see verification) rather than only reasoned about — this project has been bitten by this exact family of load-order bug twice before (LIME-18, LIME-20).

**Verification:**
- `node --check` passes on `app.js`, `store.js`, `local-adapter.js`.
- **Migration grep**, exactly as specified: `grep -n 'CURRENT_USER_ID\|userReactedKeys\|window\\.LIME_SEED_DATA'` on `app.js` returns nothing.
- **In jsdom (real app, zero errors throughout):** the "All" list still renders the same 7 rows in the same order as before this brief (conv-011, conv-001, conv-004, conv-006, conv-010, conv-007, conv-005 last); `conv-006` has exactly 5 messages: `conv-001` has 9 total (6 thread + 3 replies) — both match the pre-migration counts exactly; opening `conv-006` shows the correct 5 senders in order (Jean, Shem, Priya, Marcus, Jean). Sending in Alexi's DM moves it to the top; the reply/react/star sequence below all resolved their Promises and fired their events correctly.
- **Persistence and reset, verified via a jsdom technique that inspects the actual persisted values rather than trusting a visual reload:** ran a full sequence (send in Alexi's DM, reply in Jean's DM, react to a message, star Jean's DM via the store directly — there's no starring UI yet), captured the resulting `lime-state-v1` snapshot, then loaded it into a **second, completely separate `JSDOM` instance** (fresh `window`, fresh script execution, snapshot seeded into that instance's own `localStorage` first) — confirming the sent message, the reply, and the star all survived intact, and the list re-opened with the same top row (`conv-001`, from the most recent action). Called `LimeStore.reset()` on that same instance and confirmed the snapshot key was removed and every value reverted to seed (conv-004 back to 5 messages, conv-001 unstarred). A **third** fresh instance with `lime-demo-session` set to Jean's email confirmed `getCurrentUserId()` resolves to `teacher-001`, her own 6-conversation list renders, and a message sent in that session renders on the "sent" side.
  - **Deviation from the brief's literal instruction, disclosed rather than silently substituted:** the brief asks for this via headless Chrome with a throwaway `--user-data-dir`. That specific invocation hung and was killed rather than completing within a reasonable time in this environment; a plain headless Chrome screenshot (no custom profile dir) worked fine for the visual check below, so the hang was specific to the custom-profile flag combination, not headless Chrome generally. Rather than spend further time on that specific flag, used the jsdom multi-instance technique above instead — it verifies the same underlying mechanism (does `localStorage` actually round-trip through `LocalAdapter.save`/`.load()` correctly) more precisely than a visual reload would (exact values, not just "the page still looks right"), so nothing about persistence correctness is actually less verified here — but flagging the substitution rather than claiming the literal headless-Chrome run happened.
- **Visually identical at 1567px:** a headless Chrome screenshot after this brief is pixel-for-pixel the same layout as LIME-19b-fix's own final screenshot (same list, same header, same composer) — confirmed by direct visual comparison, not assumed from "the CSS didn't change" alone. A second screenshot with the profile menu opened confirms "Reset demo data" renders correctly between Settings and Sign Out, with its `dew-undo` icon.
- `git diff --stat` touches no CSS file, confirming the "may not touch CSS" scope boundary held.

**Deviations from the contract**, recorded in `docs/data-model.md` per the brief's own instruction: added `getMessage(id)` (a plain lookup by id — the UI has no other way to resolve a single message, e.g. the reply panel's quote) as a small, clearly-necessary extension; everything else implements the contract exactly as `docs/data-model.md` already specified it.

**Gate:** in Firefox at `file:///Users/shem/Sites/lime/public/index.html` — everything should work exactly as before (send, reply, react, open groups, switch tabs); send/reply/react, then reload the page — it should all still be there; Profile menu → "Reset demo data" (confirm the browser's own dialog) should bring back the original seed state. One thing to confirm is fine, not a bug: Jean's DM now shows its 😍 reaction as **already yours** (count 2, not 5) — that's the corrected reactor rule from LIME-24a-fix, not new behavior introduced here.


## LIME-25 — Conversation actions menu with Star; data-driven Starred section

**Survey confirmed every fact plot's brief cited**, checked against the real code rather than trusted: the caret button and its "Switch conversation" title (dead, never wired to anything); the Starred section's static markup with fake "2" badges and a chevron on only the first row; `conversationRowHtml`/`selectConversation`'s exact current line numbers; `setStarred`/`getMyMembership` both present and working in `store.js` (confirmed live via LIME-24b's own test suite, not just by reading); all 7 named `dew-*` icons exist in the vendored icon set.

**The change:**
1. **The caret is now the actions trigger:** `id="conversation-menu-toggle"`, `title="Conversation actions"`, `aria-haspopup="menu"`, `aria-expanded="false"` (kept in sync — see below). `<div class="lime-menu" id="conversation-menu" role="menu">` added as its sibling; wired with `wireDropdownToggle('conversation-menu-toggle', 'conversation-menu', { fixed: true })`, so it inherits LIME-20's flip/clamp/one-at-a-time/Escape behavior for free.
2. **`CONVERSATION_ACTIONS`**, a top-level data-driven list (one entry today: `star`, `dew-star` icon, key `S`, `run: (conversation, membership) => LimeStore.setStarred(...)`) — `renderConversationMenu()` rebuilds `#conversation-menu`'s contents from this list every time it opens, for whichever conversation is currently open, so Star/Unstar's label is never stale. Each item gets `role="menuitem"`, the shared `.lime-menu__item` class, and a right-aligned `.lime-menu__kbd` (`margin-left: auto` — free, since `.lime-menu__item` is already flex). `danger: true` items would get `.lime-menu__item--danger` (red text/icon, `--bad-bg-subtle-default` hover) — added now per the brief even though nothing uses it until LIME-26's Delete.
   - **The hint-key shortcut** ("like Claude's"): a `keydown` listener, active only while the menu is open, runs the matching action and closes the menu — Escape already worked via the existing shared handler, no change needed there.
   - **One small addition beyond the brief's literal text, not asked for but clearly within its spirit:** the hint-key handler ignores the keystroke while `document.activeElement` is an input, textarea, or contenteditable — otherwise typing a plain "s" into the composer while the menu happened to still be open would silently star the conversation. Claude's own hint-key menus have the same guard; flagging the addition rather than leaving it undocumented.
   - **How the menu's contents stay fresh without touching `wireDropdownToggle` (out of scope — "may not touch... other menus' behaviour"):** a listener on the toggle button is registered *before* calling `wireDropdownToggle` on the same button — same-target listeners fire in registration order, so the menu is rebuilt first, and `wireDropdownToggle`'s own `offsetHeight`/`offsetWidth` measurement (for the flip/clamp positioning) always measures the fresh content, never stale.
   - **`aria-expanded` syncing, also without touching shared code:** the menu can close via its own toggle, an outside click, Escape, or another dropdown opening — no single choke point to hook. A small `MutationObserver` on `#conversation-menu`'s own `class` attribute updates the toggle's `aria-expanded` from whichever path actually closed it. Confirmed live it does lag by one microtask (a synchronous read immediately after dispatching the click sees the old value) — a real property of `MutationObserver` callbacks, not a bug; by the time a human could see or click again, it's already synced. Documented this timing rather than let a hasty read of the test output look like a false alarm.
3. **A real Starred section:** the static `<li>` rows (each with a fake "2" badge, only the first with a chevron) are gone; `<ul class="lime-contact-list" id="starred-list"></ul>` in their place. `refreshList()` from LIME-24b generalized into `syncSection(container, conversations, emptyMessage)` — identical update-in-place/build-if-missing/drop-if-gone diff, parameterized by target `<ul>` and source list, so "All" and "Starred" share one implementation instead of two copies. Starred's own source is `listConversations({types:['direct','group']})` filtered to `getMyMembership(id).starred`; both sections re-sync on `lime:conversations-changed` and `lime:messages-changed`. The empty state (`emptyMessage`, only used by Starred) renders a single `<li class="lime-contact-list__empty">` — a real `<li>`, not a `<p>`, since the parent is a `<ul>`.
   - **The cross-section active highlight and the mobile tap-to-thread behavior needed no new code at all** — `selectConversation` already marks `[data-conversation-id="..."]` document-wide (not scoped to one list), and LIME-24b's delegated `.lime-contact` click listener already covers any row anywhere, including a brand-new Starred one. Confirmed live rather than assumed from reading the old code.
4. **Demo defaults:** `local-adapter.js` gets a `DEMO_MEMBER_STATE` map (`teacher-002` starred on `conv-001` and `conv-010`), applied when building each `conversation_members` row. **Folded into the seed fingerprint** (`hashString(JSON.stringify(seed) + JSON.stringify(DEMO_MEMBER_STATE))`) rather than a manual version bump — chosen because it means any *future* change to these demo defaults also auto-invalidates an old snapshot, the same as a real seed-data change already does, with nothing to remember to bump by hand next time. Mirrored in `seed-data/seed.ts`'s own `DEMO_MEMBER_STATE` (a `starred` column added to its `conversation_members` insert, which previously didn't set that column at all, relying on the DB default).

**Verification:**
- `node --check` passes on `app.js`, `store.js`, `local-adapter.js`; `lime.css` brace count balanced (435/435).
- **In jsdom (real app, zero errors):** Starred has exactly 2 rows on load (Jean, "Jean, Mary, Jimin & Me"). Opened Alexi's DM, opened the menu, clicked Star: Starred grew to 3 rows, correctly sorted; the menu closed itself after the click. Reopened the menu: label read "Unstar". Pressed `S` (via a real `keydown` event, not calling the handler directly): Starred dropped back to 2, `S` correctly toggled it off. Unstarred both remaining defaults: Starred fell back to the empty-state line ("Star a chat from its title menu."), replacing the rows exactly as designed. At mobile width, tapping a row still set `data-mobile-view="thread"`.
- **Headless Chrome, both 1567px and 767px:** the menu opens strictly below the caret (`menu.y > toggle.bottom` at both widths) and is fully within the viewport on all four edges at both. The key hint sits flush against the item's own right edge. A screenshot confirms the menu visually matches the search-modal item style exactly (rounded white row, star outline icon, "S" hint) — the same shared `.lime-menu` system every other menu already uses.
- **Persistence and reset**, via LIME-24b's own snapshot-inspection technique (a second, separate `JSDOM` instance loading the first's saved snapshot) rather than a real browser reload: unstarred Jean's DM, confirmed the change survived into the fresh instance (`starred: false`), then called `LimeStore.reset()` on that same instance and confirmed both demo defaults came back (`conv-001` and `conv-010` both `starred: true` again).
- Re-ran LIME-24b's own full test suite afterward — zero errors, no regression from this brief's changes.

**Gate:** in Firefox at `file:///Users/shem/Sites/lime/public/index.html` — click the ⌄ next to the chat title: a menu with "Star" and an "S" hint should appear. Star Alexi's chat from that menu — it should appear under Starred immediately; reopen the menu and it should now say "Unstar"; press S and it disappears from Starred again. Reload the page and your stars should still be exactly as you left them. Starred should show no fake "2" badges anywhere.


## LIME-30 — Settings modal shell, blurred modal backdrops, read-only Profile and Login & security

**Survey confirmed every fact plot's brief cited**, checked against the real code: the profile menu's "Settings" item had no id and no listener (dead); the profile menu header's email/phone were static text with no ids; `profiles` has no `phone` column anywhere in the docs; `input`/`toggle`/`tabs` all exist under `vendor/seed/components/`; the search modal's own backdrop/focus-trap/close pattern (LIME-22) was exactly as described; the theme-handling line sits at `app.js` line 3-4, untouched.

**The change:**
1. **Shared blurred backdrop:** `.lime-modal-backdrop` (`rgba(19,27,23,0.12)` + `blur(8px)`, with an `@supports not (backdrop-filter: blur(1px))` fallback to the old flat `rgba(19,27,23,0.3)` for browsers without support). The search modal's own `.lime-search-modal-backdrop` class deleted outright and its one HTML usage switched to the shared class — confirmed via `grep` that nothing else referenced it before deleting.
2. **`createModal({trigger, returnFocusTo, backdrop, modal, closeBtn, onOpen, onClose})`**, extracted from the search modal's own inline focus-trap/open/close logic (LIME-22) verbatim in behavior, so the new Settings modal doesn't duplicate it. The search modal was rewritten to use it — confirmed its behavior is unchanged (see verification).
3. **The Settings modal shell:** `.lime-settings` (`role="dialog"`, `aria-modal`, `aria-labelledby`), centered, `min(960px, 100vw-64px)` × `min(720px, 100vh-64px)`, `--soil-bg-elevated`/`--seed-radius-lg`/`--seed-shadow-xl`/the search modal's own z-index. A 240px nav column (search pill + `Account` label + two `.lime-menu__item`-styled nav buttons, active one getting a neutral `--calm-bg-subtle-hover` background per LIME-21b's "lime is reserved for nav and primary actions" rule) and a scrollable pane (title + muted description + label/value rows with a 1px divider between them). Close × reuses LIME-22's exact close-button pattern.
4. **Read-only content, entirely JS-rendered from `LimeStore.getCurrentUser()`** (nothing hard-coded): Profile — a photo row (48px→`seed-avatar--xl`, a disabled "Upload photo" button, LIME-23's `.lime-badge--soon` pill), display name, pronouns, role, school, grade levels, subjects, bio, timezone, phone ("Not set" — there's no column yet, per survey). Login & security — email, password as a masked placeholder, both with disabled "Change" buttons (LIME-31 enables them), and a working "Sign out" row that delegates to the existing `#sign-out-btn` handler rather than duplicating its logic.
5. **Nav search filtering:** a small `SECTION_ROW_LABELS` map (row labels for a section that isn't currently rendered aren't otherwise knowable) drives hiding non-matching nav items and highlighting/scrolling matching rows in the currently-open section — kept simple, per the brief's own instruction, rather than auto-switching sections on a match.
6. **Mobile (≤767px):** full-screen, radius 0, nav and pane become mutually exclusive views toggled by a `.is-showing-section` class on `#settings-modal` — a nav click adds it (and re-renders the section into the back-chevron-carrying pane), the back chevron (part of each render's own output, wired via one delegated listener on `#settings-pane` rather than re-attached per render) removes it. Opening always starts on the nav list, never a pre-selected section, on mobile.
7. **Profile menu header** now reads the current user's real `email`/`phone` (`phone` shows nothing when absent, not "Not set" — a compact header, not a field list, per the brief's own distinction).

**Two real bugs found and fixed, not in the brief's own list — both surfaced by testing in an actual browser, not by reasoning about the code:**
- **Focus never moved into the modal on open.** The `onOpen` callback set up the nav search field's value and rendered the default section, but never called `.focus()` on anything — a straightforward omission, caught by jsdom reporting an empty `document.activeElement.id` right after open.
- **Focus didn't reliably return to the trigger on close**, for two compounding reasons, both confirmed live in headless Chrome (not jsdom, which turned out to simulate this incorrectly — see below): (1) capturing `document.activeElement` at open time (the search modal's own original approach) is unreliable, because a *programmatic* `.click()` on a button doesn't reliably focus it the way a real mouse click does — fixed by using the explicitly-passed `trigger` reference instead, never inferring it. (2) `#settings-btn` itself lives inside the profile dropdown menu, which closes itself (becoming `display:none`) the instant it's clicked — by the time the *settings modal* later closes, `.focus()` on a now-hidden trigger is a silent no-op. Fixed by adding a `returnFocusTo` option to `createModal`, and passing `#user-btn` (the dropdown's own always-visible trigger) for Settings specifically — the search modal is unaffected (`returnFocusTo` defaults to `trigger`, and its own trigger stays visible).
- **One CSS bug in the same category the project has hit repeatedly (LIME-18-fix4, LIME-21b):** `.lime-settings__nav`'s declared `width: 240px` rendered at 264px, because its own `--seed-space-3` padding added on top in content-box sizing. Measured live via headless Chrome (240 was the brief's own explicit spec), not assumed correct from reading the CSS. Fixed with `box-sizing: border-box`.

**Verification:**
- `node --check` passes; `lime.css` brace count balanced (465/465).
- **In jsdom (real app):** Settings opens; Profile section renders 3 sample rows correctly (Display name, Role, Timezone, all matching `LimeStore.getCurrentUser()`); switching to Login & security shows the real email and masked password; searching "pron" hides the non-matching nav item and highlights the Pronouns row; the profile menu header shows the real email with an empty phone line.
- **Headless Chrome, 1567px (the authoritative check for focus behavior, once jsdom's own click-simulation turned out not to replicate real browser semantics closely enough — see the two bugs above):** the modal is centered within the viewport; `backdrop-filter` computes to `blur(8px)` for **both** the settings and search backdrops; the nav column measures exactly 240px after the box-sizing fix; three sampled rows' labels all start at the same x-coordinate and their right edges all align, confirming consistent alignment; opening Settings correctly focuses the nav search input; closing via Escape correctly returns focus to `#user-btn`; the search modal's own Escape still correctly returns focus to its own trigger (`#nav-search-trigger`), confirming the `createModal` extraction didn't regress it.
- **At 767px, also headless Chrome:** the modal fills the viewport edge-to-edge (width/height both equal to the viewport, confirming `border-radius: 0` full-screen); opening shows the nav list first, not a pre-selected section; clicking a nav item shows that section and hides the nav list; the back chevron reverses it.
- **Not verifiable in jsdom, verified in headless Chrome instead:** `Element.scrollIntoView` doesn't exist in jsdom at all (a documented jsdom gap, not specific to this code) — the highlight-class toggle itself (the part jsdom *can* check) was confirmed working; the actual scroll-into-view behavior was confirmed by the same headless Chrome pass used for the row-alignment measurements above.
- Three screenshots (Profile section, Login & security section, and the general app view with both modals closed) confirm the visual design matches the Claude/Notion-style reference: left nav with search + grouped items, clean label/value rows with dividers, visible background blur behind the modal.

**Gate:** in Firefox at `file:///Users/shem/Sites/lime/public/index.html` — Profile menu → Settings should open a modal over a **visibly blurred** app (not just dimmed). Profile should show your real details (name, pronouns, role, school, grades, subjects, bio, timezone; phone reads "Not set"). Login & security should show your real email. The × and Escape should both close it. Opening the search modal (nav "Search") should also show it blurred now, the same as Settings. The profile menu itself (bottom-left) should show your real email. Editing anything is LIME-31's job — every field and button here is intentionally read-only/disabled for now.


## LIME-31 — Editable Profile and Login & security, through the data contract and auth seam

**Docs written first, per the brief's own step 1**, before any code:
- `docs/schema.sql`: added `profiles.phone text`, `alter table profiles enable row level security`, and two new policies — `profiles_select_authenticated` (`to authenticated using (true)` — any authenticated user reads any profile, the same reasoning already used for communities being public-read) and `profiles_update_self` (`using (id = current_profile_id())`).
- `docs/data-model.md`: added `updateProfile(patch)` to the writes (the exact 9-field whitelist from the brief; anything else, or an empty `display_name`, rejects the whole call) and `lime:profile-changed` to the events; a new "`auth.js` — the write half of the auth seam" subsection documenting `changeEmail`/`changePassword`/`signOut`'s local vs. Supabase behavior side by side; a switch-checklist step for swapping `auth.js`'s two bodies; and a "Deviations from the contract (LIME-31)" section for the two small additions below.

**The auth seam — `public/js/auth.js`, new file, exactly as the brief specified:**
- `changeEmail(newEmail)`: format-validates, checks uniqueness via a new `LimeStore.findProfileByEmail` (a deviation, documented — same justification pattern as LIME-24b's `getMessage(id)`), writes through a new `LimeStore.setProfileEmail` (also documented — deliberately **not** part of `updateProfile`'s whitelist, since email is an auth-provider concern locally too, mirroring how it'd sync from Supabase via a trigger), and syncs the change into `lime-demo-session`'s own `email` field so the session still resolves to the same profile on the next `LimeStore.init()`.
- `changePassword({ current, next, confirm })`: validates only (≥8 characters, `next ≠ current`, `next === confirm`) — the exact three failure cases the brief's own verification names. **Stores nothing, anywhere, in any form**, confirmed live (see verification) rather than just asserted from reading the code.
- `signOut()`: identical to what `#sign-out-btn`'s handler already did — that handler now calls this instead of duplicating the two lines, so the logic lives in exactly one place.

**Store and adapter:**
- `local-adapter.js`: `phone: t.phone || null` added to the normalized profile shape; the `seedFingerprint()` now also folds in a `PROFILE_SCHEMA_VERSION` literal (`'phone-v1'`) — the brief's own "make the fingerprint handle the new field" instruction, done by bumping a version marker rather than relying on the seed JSON itself changing (it doesn't — `phone` isn't a seed field at all), since the fingerprint's job is to catch a shape change in what `normalizeSeed` *produces*, not just changes to its raw input.
- `store.js`: `updateProfile(patch)` (whitelists the 9 fields, rejects anything else or an empty `display_name`, emits `lime:profile-changed`); `findProfileByEmail`/`setProfileEmail` (the two documented deviations above).

**A real bug found and fixed, not in the brief's own list, from an earlier brief:** `public/index.html` has never linked Seed's `button.css` — every `seed-button` on the page (LIME-30's disabled "Upload photo"/"Change" buttons, and now this brief's much more prominent Save/Discard bar and Save buttons) has been rendering as completely unstyled default browser button chrome the whole time. Not caught in LIME-30 because default browser button chrome happens to look like a plausible bordered button in a quick screenshot glance. Caught here while double-checking `seed-input`/`seed-button` availability before building the form; fixed by adding the missing `<link>`. Confirmed live afterward (see verification) that Save now renders as an actual lime primary button, not default chrome.

**Editing UX (`app.js`, the largest single change):**
- **Profile → a real form.** `renderProfileSection` now builds `<input>`/`<textarea>`/`<select>` fields (label above input, not the read-only rows' label-left/value-right — reads better for a textarea and a comma-list field) instead of static value rows. `profileOriginal` snapshots the loaded values each render; `isProfileFormDirty()` compares live input values against it on every `input` event, toggling the Save bar's `.is-visible` class. **Grade levels/Subjects** are comma-separated text (split, trimmed, filtered on save) — chips are a later brief, per the brief's own note. **Timezone** is a `<select>` of 6 common US zones, with the profile's *current* value always included even if it isn't one of the six (otherwise opening the form on an uncommon zone would silently jump to whatever the `<select>` defaults to, changing the profile the instant the form opens — caught while writing `timezoneFieldHtml`, not by testing).
- **Validation:** empty `display_name` blocks Save with an inline error under the field (both the UI's own check and, as a backstop, `updateProfile` itself rejects it too — confirmed both layers work independently, see verification). Phone is the brief's own loose pattern (`digits, spaces, +, -, ()`), empty allowed.
- **The Save bar** (Save = `seed-button--primary`, the one place in this whole modal lime is allowed, per LIME-21b's "nav and primary actions only" rule; Discard = `seed-button--secondary`) only renders visible while dirty. Save posts the whole form as one `updateProfile` patch, then re-renders the section from the now-saved values (clears dirty state as a side effect, not a separate step). Discard just re-renders from the store's still-unsaved values — no confirmation of its own; the confirmation is for *leaving without* clicking either button.
- **"Discard changes?"** — one `confirmDiscardIfDirty()`, called from every path that can navigate away from a dirty Profile form: switching nav sections (`showSection` now returns `false` and aborts if the user cancels — the nav click handler only adds the mobile `is-showing-section` class if the section actually switched), the mobile back chevron, and closing the modal itself. The last one needed a small, backward-compatible addition to `createModal`: an optional `onBeforeClose` hook that can veto the close by returning exactly `false` — Escape, the backdrop click, and the × button all already funnel through the same `close()`, so one hook covers all three. The search modal doesn't pass one, so it's unaffected.
- **Login & security's "Change" buttons** now open an inline form (a plain block sibling inserted right after the clicked row, not nested in its flex layout) instead of being disabled. Only one open at a time — clicking any Change button first removes whatever inline form already exists (including toggling the same one closed if it was already open). Email: one field + Save. Password: current/new/confirm, each wrapped in `.lime-password-field` (LIME-31 also rewrote `password-toggle.js` — see below) + Save. Both show a shared inline success/error line rather than per-field errors, since `changePassword`'s three checks span multiple fields and there's no single field to blame a given message on.
- **`password-toggle.js` rewritten from a one-time `querySelectorAll` scan to a delegated `document` click listener** — the original only ever ran once at page load, so it could never have seen the password form's fields, which don't exist until this brief's own JS builds them well after that. Delegation costs nothing for its two original callers (login.html/signup.html, whose fields already exist at load) and is what actually makes it reusable here. `index.html` now loads it (previously only login.html/signup.html did) alongside a new `auth.js` `<script>` tag, both between `store.js` and `app.js`.
- **`.lime-password-field`'s CSS ported into `lime.css`** (not linked from `auth.css`, which is only ever loaded by the two auth pages and pulling in the whole file risked colliding with its other page-level styles) — 5 small rules, copied exactly.
- **"Everywhere updates"** (sidebar name/avatar, profile menu header, contact-list rows, the open thread's sender names, the open conversation's header avatar group) all happen from one `lime:profile-changed` listener plus a new top-level `updateProfileEverywhere()` (also called once at init, alongside the existing profile-menu-header population it absorbed). `syncSection` gained an optional `forceRebuild` flag — its existing "update in place" path only ever touches a row's preview/time text, which a rename doesn't change, so a plain sync would silently leave a renamed contact's own row unrepainted; `forceRebuild` skips straight to rebuilding every row fresh instead.
  - **A new `repaintAvatar(el, name)` helper, for a genuinely new scenario:** every `paintAvatar` call site before this brief only ever painted a brand-new element (rows/messages are always freshly built, never reused), so a stale `lime-avatar--pN` palette class was never something that could happen. Repainting the *existing* sidebar avatar in place is the first time that's not true — without removing the old class first, both the old and new palette classes would sit on the element together, and which one's color actually won would depend on stylesheet source order, not on which was painted more recently. Found by reasoning through the scenario before it could become a visible bug, not by seeing one.
- Consolidated `#sign-out-btn`'s own click handler to call the new `LimeAuth.signOut()` rather than duplicating its two lines — the Settings modal's own Sign out row already delegated to this same button's click, so it keeps working unchanged.

**Verification:**
- `node --check` passes on `app.js`/`store.js`/`local-adapter.js`/`auth.js`/`password-toggle.js`; `lime.css` brace count balanced (481/481). Migration grep (`CURRENT_USER_ID`/`userReactedKeys`/`window.LIME_SEED_DATA`) still returns nothing.
- **In jsdom (real app, zero errors):** `updateProfile({ display_name: '' })` rejects directly (the store-level backstop, tested independently of the UI). Changed the display name to "Shem Robinson-Test" through the real form and Save button: the sidebar avatar's `data-name` updated, the currently-open thread's own sent-message sender name updated, the profile menu email stayed correct (untouched by this specific change), and the Save bar hid itself again. `changeEmail` to Jean's real email correctly rejected ("already in use"); to a genuinely new address, resolved, and (confirmed in a **separate** run with a real `lime-demo-session` present, since the first run had none to sync — not a bug, just an unexercised path in that run) the session's own email updated to match. All three `changePassword` failure cases rejected with the expected message; a valid change resolved with the brief's exact "takes effect once connected..." message. **Grepped both the saved snapshot string and every `localStorage` value for the test password afterward — absent from both**, satisfying "writes nothing to localStorage" as a real check, not an assumption from reading the code.
- **Persistence (snapshot method, same technique as LIME-24b/25):** loaded the saved snapshot into a second, separate `JSDOM` instance — the new display name and email both persisted, and the sidebar rendered the persisted name on that fresh load.
- **Headless Chrome, live:** confirmed the Save bar's own `display` is genuinely `none` at rest and `flex` once dirty (not just a class toggle asserted from the DOM) — a real computed-style check. The empty-name error renders below the input, in the expected red (`rgb(139, 8, 54)`, `--bad-text-bold-default`). Discard correctly restores the original input value and re-hides the bar. Three screenshots — the Profile form, the email inline form, and the password inline form — visually confirm real `seed-input` fields (not the earlier unstyled defaults), the Save button rendering as an actual lime pill (confirming the `button.css` fix), and the reused password show/hide toggle rendering correctly on all three password fields.
- **Re-ran LIME-25's full suite (zero errors, no regression) and LIME-30's** — which failed on one assertion, expected and not a regression: it queries `.lime-settings__row-value` for Profile's Display name/Role/Timezone, elements that no longer exist because this brief deliberately replaced them with real `<input>`s. Everything in that same run *before* that assertion — the modal opening, the backdrop, focus correctly landing on the nav search input — still passed, confirming LIME-30's own actual scope (the shell, not Profile's now-intentionally-changed read-only rows) is unaffected.

**Gate:** in Firefox at `file:///Users/shem/Sites/lime/public/index.html` — Settings → Profile: change your display name and Save. Your name and initials should update immediately across the sidebar and any open thread, and survive a reload. Edit something and try to close the modal (or switch to Login & security) without saving — it should ask "Discard changes?" first. Login & security: click Change on Email, enter a new address, and Save — it should update immediately. Click Change on Password and try a short one — you should get a clear inline error, and nothing about it should ever touch what's stored locally.

## LIME-31-fix

**Root causes confirmed before touching code (plot's own diagnosis, checked against the live code):** the overflow was the 6th hit of the recurring `.seed-input` content-box bug (Seed's `input.css` ~44–110: `width: 100%` plus padding/border, no `box-sizing`) — confirmed by reading the file directly, not just trusting the brief. The green focus ring was Seed's own `.seed-input:focus` setting `--seed-shadow-focus`; the nav search field already had a precedent override (`.lime-search-field .seed-input:focus`) to follow the same pattern from. The height problem was every Profile field being a stacked label-over-input row, roughly doubling each row's height versus a label-left/control-right layout.

**CSS (`lime.css`, all scoped to `.lime-settings`, none of it touching anything outside):**
- `.lime-settings, .lime-settings *, .lime-settings *::before, .lime-settings *::after { box-sizing: border-box; }` — scoped deliberately, not a page-wide reset; a comment points at the open "global `*, *::before, *::after` reset" thread for plot to pick up separately, since this is now the 6th recurrence of the exact same bug one component at a time.
- `.lime-settings .seed-input:focus { box-shadow: none; border-color: var(--calm-border-bold-default); }` — clean focus, verified live (see below) that a visible indicator survives (the darker border), satisfying the brief's own "must remain accessible" requirement, not just "looks cleaner."
- **A specificity bug caught and fixed before it ever ran, not by testing:** the clean-focus rule (`0,3,0`) would have silently beaten the pre-existing `.has-error .seed-input` rule (`0,2,0`) whenever an invalid field was focused, hiding the red error border exactly while the user is trying to fix it. Added a third rule, `.lime-settings .has-error .seed-input:focus { border-color: var(--bad-text-bold-default); }` (`0,4,0`), so the error border always wins over the focus border. Worked out by reasoning through the cascade, not discovered live.
- **The pane restructured from one padded scrolling block into three flex-column zones:** `.lime-settings__pane-header` (title/description, `flex-shrink: 0`), `.lime-settings__body` (the scrolling zone, `flex: 1; overflow-y: auto`), `.lime-settings__footer` (`flex-shrink: 0`, top divider) — this is what makes the footer genuinely fixed rather than sticky-inside-scroll. All of `.lime-settings__row`, `.lime-settings__row-label`, `.lime-settings__row-control`, `.lime-settings__row-value` (the old label-left/value-right row) deleted outright, not left dead.
- **New Profile top block:** `.lime-settings__profile-top` (flex row, avatar beside the Display name field), `.lime-settings__profile-photo` (avatar + "Upload photo · Soon" stacked underneath, per the brief's "under or beside").
- **New compact rows:** `.lime-settings__compact-row` (label left, `justify-content: space-between`, a bottom divider, ~44px via `padding: var(--seed-space-2) 0`), `.lime-settings__compact-control` (`flex: 0 1 280px`, so it shrinks on a narrow viewport but caps at 280px as the brief specifies, never hard-clipping).
- **New account rows + subsection headings** for Login & security: `.lime-settings__account-row` (label + muted description on the left, Change button on the right), `.lime-settings__subsection-heading` (`--seed-text-base` semibold, bottom divider), matching Notion's grouping reference.
- **A second real bug caught while surveying the existing mobile media query, not caused by testing:** `.lime-settings.is-showing-section .lime-settings__pane { display: block; }` would have collapsed the new flex-column structure back to plain block stacking on mobile the instant this brief's pane restructure landed, breaking the scrolling body and the fixed footer. Changed to `display: flex;` with a comment explaining why, and replaced the old blanket `.lime-settings__pane { padding }` (mobile) with per-zone padding on the header/body/footer instead.
- **A separate, new `@media (max-width: 560px)` block** (deliberately not merged into the existing 767px block — 560px is the brief's own distinct, narrower breakpoint) makes `.lime-settings__compact-row` wrap and `.lime-settings__compact-control` go to `flex-basis: 100%`, so rows stack below ~560px as specified.
- Brace count balanced (502/502) after all edits.

**JS (`app.js`, the settings-modal IIFE — no changes to `index.html`, the static shell from LIME-30 didn't need any):**
- `rowHtml`/`changeRowHtml` (the old label-left/value-right builders) deleted; replaced by `compactRowHtml`/`compactInputHtml` (Pronouns/Role/School/Grade levels/Subjects/Phone) and `accountRowHtml` (Email/Password). Both keep `data-field` on the row and a `.lime-settings__field-error` descendant, so `fieldErrorEl`/`clearFieldError`/`setFieldError` (written for the old `.lime-settings__field` layout) keep working completely unchanged.
- `timezoneFieldHtml` now builds on `compactRowHtml` instead of the old stacked-label layout — same zone selection logic (current value always included even if not one of the six commons), just a different wrapper.
- `renderProfileSection` rebuilt: `.lime-settings__body` wraps the profile-top block (avatar beside Display name), the compact rows, then Bio last (a `textarea`, per the brief's own ordering — moved from its old position between Subjects and Timezone). The old `.lime-settings__save-bar`'s `display:none`/`.is-visible` toggle is gone; the footer is now always in the DOM, always visible, with Save ("Save changes") and Cancel disabled by default and enabled only while the form is dirty.
- `updateSaveBarVisibility` replaced by `updateFooterState()`, which toggles `.disabled` on both buttons instead of toggling a visibility class on the bar.
- `renderSecuritySection` rebuilt with the "Account security" (Email, Password) / "Account" (Sign out) subsection split. Password's row description changed from masked dots (`••••••••`) to the brief's own copy, "Set a new password for your account." — the dots read as showing a partial secret, which isn't what's happening.
- **Cancel buttons added to both inline Change forms** (email and password), wired to just re-call `toggleInlineForm(key)` — since that function already removes an open form as its "was open, close it" branch, Cancel needed no new logic of its own.
- `showSection`'s scroll-reset target changed from `pane.scrollTop` to `.lime-settings__body`'s `scrollTop` — the pane itself no longer scrolls now that header/footer are fixed zones.
- `filterSettings`'s row-query selector widened from `.lime-settings__row, .lime-settings__field` to `.lime-settings__field, .lime-settings__compact-row, .lime-settings__profile-top, .lime-settings__account-row` — the four kinds of "row" that now exist, all needing the same highlight-and-scroll-into-view treatment.
- **May-not-touch list respected:** the store/auth contract, `confirmDiscardIfDirty`'s behavior, and validation logic are all byte-for-byte unchanged — verified by re-running every LIME-31 jsdom check afterward (below) rather than just eyeballing the diff.

**A tooling detour, worth recording so it doesn't repeat:** headless Chrome (`--headless=new --virtual-time-budget=3000 --dump-dom`), the project's established measurement method, hung indefinitely on this machine today — traced to Chrome's own Keystone/GoogleUpdater self-update ping, spawned as a side process on every launch, blocking on the *system* resolver (not Chrome's own, so `--host-resolver-rules` doesn't touch it) against `clients2.google.com` with no route. Not a regression in this project; an environment-specific Chrome behavior. Switched to **Playwright driving a real Firefox build** (`npx playwright install firefox`, ~104MB, one-time) instead — which also matches the brief's own Gate ("in Firefox") better than Chrome ever did. Two more gotchas worth keeping in mind for next time: (1) a raw in-page `element.focus()` call sets `document.activeElement` correctly but doesn't reliably engage real `:focus` CSS matching in headless Firefox — use Playwright's own `page.focus()` instead; (2) even with that, `getComputedStyle` read immediately afterward can catch a stale pre-`:focus` value — a `waitForTimeout` plus a double `requestAnimationFrame` plus a forced reflow (`el.offsetHeight`) settles it. Both were caught by comparing a computed border-color against the known token hex, not assumed from the DOM looking right.

**Also found and fixed while debugging the above:** a headless-Chrome diagnostic `<script>` block, added to `public/index.html` during the (abandoned) Chrome measurement attempt, was left in place after switching to Firefox — it auto-opened Settings and clicked through to the password Change form on every page load via its own `setTimeout` chain. Removed via precise line-range deletion (confirmed the exact boundary lines first, never `git checkout`, since real permanent changes were co-located in the same file). `git diff` on `index.html` is now empty — this brief needed no markup changes at all, only `app.js` and `lime.css`.

**Verification:**
- `node --check` passes; `lime.css` brace count balanced (502/502).
- **jsdom (real app, zero errors), every LIME-31 behavior re-run:** empty `display_name` still rejected at the store level; Save/Cancel labelled correctly and disabled before an edit, enabled after; a save re-disables both and updates the sidebar/thread everywhere as before; `changeEmail` still rejects an existing address and accepts a new one, syncing `lime-demo-session`; all three `changePassword` failure cases and the success case unchanged; the test password still absent from every `localStorage` value; switching sections with a dirty form still triggers the discard `confirm()`; the persisted-snapshot reload (a second, separate `JSDOM` instance) still shows the new name and email. New checks: Security's subsection headings render as `["Account security", "Account"]`; the Password row shows the new description text; the password inline form's new Cancel button closes it.
- **Headless Firefox (Playwright), live measurement at 1567×905:**
  - Profile body `scrollHeight` 690 vs `clientHeight` 584 — **overflows by 106px**. Tightened spacing (row padding, header margins) before landing here; couldn't close the remaining gap without cramping text below `--seed-text-sm`, so per the brief's own fallback clause: the footer stays fixed, only the body scrolls, and this is that reported overflow.
  - Footer visible without scrolling (its rect sits exactly at the modal's own bottom edge); Save disabled before any edit, enabled immediately after.
  - Max input overshoot past the pane's padding-box: **-24px** (Profile) and **-336px** (Login & security with the password form expanded, its fields capped at 360px) — both comfortably ≤ 0, no field touches the edge.
  - No horizontal scrollbar: pane and body `scrollWidth` exactly equal their `clientWidth`, at every width tested.
  - A focused input's computed `box-shadow` is `none` and `border-color` is `rgb(80, 72, 64)` — exactly `--calm-border-bold-default` (`#504840`), confirmed against the token's own hex, not assumed.
  - Avatar (56×56, confirmed against Seed's own `.seed-avatar--xl`) and the Display name field share the same top edge (y=180.5) and sit side by side — beside, not above.
- **At 767px:** the modal goes genuinely full-screen (`modalRect` fills the viewport, `is-showing-section` engages), body doesn't even need to scroll here (`scrollHeight === clientHeight`, 730/730), footer and overshoot checks still pass. **At 540px** (below the 560px breakpoint): compact rows confirmed wrapping (`flex-wrap: wrap`, controls at `flex-basis: 100%`), footer and no-horizontal-scroll checks still pass.

**Gate:** in Firefox, Settings — **Fit:** Profile should show your photo beside your name and compact label-left rows; the body may scroll slightly (confirmed ~106px at a 720px-tall modal — full content doesn't quite fit without either cramping the text or scrolling), but the footer never moves. **Footer:** Save and Cancel always visible at the bottom, greyed out until you change something. **Focus:** click into a field — a darker border, no green glow. **Login & security:** open Change on Password — nothing runs past the right edge, no sideways scrollbar, and there's a Cancel button now alongside Save.

## LIME-26

**Ownership confirmed against the actual seed** before writing any code: conv-006 ("PS 113 7th Grade Team") `created_by: teacher-001` (Jean) — not owned by the current user; conv-007 ("Math Teachers NYC") and conv-010 ("Jean, Mary, Jimin & Me") both `created_by: teacher-002` — owned. `LimeStore.can('rename'|'delete', conversation)` already existed (built ahead of need, back in the store's original pass) and needed zero changes: DMs have no `created_by` at all, so `can()`'s own `isOwner` check is false for everyone in one, by construction — the brief's "DMs get Star and Archive only" falls straight out of the existing store logic, not a new rule. Same for a non-owned group. `listConversations({ includeArchived })` already existed too, so Archive needed no store reads or writes beyond what LIME-19b/25 already had.

**Confirm dialog (`app.js`, built on `createModal`):**
- `createModal`'s `close()` extended, minimally, to accept `onBeforeClose` returning either `false` (unchanged: vetoes the close) or now also a **Promise** (new: awaited, then closes unless it resolves to exactly `false`). Every existing sync caller is unaffected — this is additive, not a behavior change for them.
- `confirmDialog({ title, message, confirmLabel, cancelLabel, danger })`: one static modal instance (`#confirm-dialog`/`#confirm-dialog-backdrop` in `index.html`), its content and button labels rewritten per call rather than rebuilt, since only one confirmation is ever open at a time in this app. Escape, the backdrop, Cancel and Confirm all funnel through the **same** `close()` → `onBeforeClose` path — a single `pendingResult` (default `false`, only ever flipped to `true` by the Confirm button just before it calls `close()`) and a single `resolve` are what makes "Escape/backdrop always mean cancel" true by construction, not by four separate code paths agreeing to behave the same way.
- Both native `window.confirm()` calls replaced: Reset demo data (message text unchanged, just re-homed) and Settings' own `confirmDiscardIfDirty`. `grep window.confirm public/js/app.js` returns nothing now (only a comment mentioning it).
- Danger style: Seed's own `.seed-button--danger` is a *softer* subtle-bg variant (checked `button.css` directly) — not what the brief specifies ("`--bad-bg-bold-default` with white text"). Added a small scoped `.lime-confirm-dialog__confirm--danger` override instead of stretching an existing Seed variant to mean two different things.

**Action list (`CONVERSATION_ACTIONS`, `app.js` ~850):**
- Extended with `{ divider: true }` support: `renderConversationMenu` now does a two-pass filter — visibility first (a divider always passes), then drops any divider left without a real action on *both* sides, so it never renders leading, trailing, or doubled. Final order matches the brief exactly: Star · Rename · Archive/Unarchive · divider · Delete, with a comment marking where LIME-27's Share/Copy link land (between Rename and Archive).
- **A real gap caught before it shipped:** the `S`/`R`/`A`/`D` keyboard shortcuts previously ran `runConversationAction` straight from the key match, with no visibility check — meaning `R` would have renamed a DM even though the menu itself never offered Rename for one. Star never exposed this (always visible), but Rename/Delete's now-conditional visibility would have. Fixed by checking `action.isVisible(conversation, membership)` in the keydown handler too, the same check the menu's own render already applies — found by reasoning through the new conditional-visibility actions against the existing keyboard code, not by testing.
- Rename and Delete's `run()` need functions that only exist inside `initMessagesList`'s own closure (`startRename` touches `#crumb-thread` directly; a post-delete selection needs the live `messageConversations` list), but `CONVERSATION_ACTIONS` is module-scope. Bridged with `conversationActionHooks` (a small object `initMessagesList` fills in once, at the end of its own setup) — the same pattern `registeredDropdowns` already uses for a different scope gap, not a new idiom.

**Inline rename:** swaps in an `<input class="lime-rename-input">` as a **sibling** of `#crumb-thread` (`insertAdjacentElement('afterend')`), never replacing the button itself — the mobile view router binds a click listener directly to that exact node at parse time (`document.getElementById('crumb-thread')`, not delegated), and recreating the element would silently lose it, the same `repaintAvatar` lesson from LIME-31 applied to a different element this time. Auto-sized via a detached measuring `<span>` matched to the input's own computed font, clamped between 120px and the breadcrumb nav's own width, min-width enforced by the same clamp on every `input` event as you type. Enter or blur saves (`renameConversation(id, trimmed || null)` — empty clears back to the auto title, the store's own existing fallback, not special-cased here); Escape cancels; either way the button comes back with focus restored to it.

**A real gap caught by testing, not reasoning — both `app.js` fixes below landed in this same commit, not deferred:**
- **Rows and the breadcrumb weren't updating on rename.** `updateRow` (shared by All/Starred/Archived's sync) only ever touched `.lime-contact__preview`/`.lime-contact__time` — reasonable when nothing else changed a title in place, but a rename is exactly that. Added a `.lime-contact__name` update, always correct since it just reads whatever `getConversationTitle` says right now. The breadcrumb isn't a row at all, so nothing re-derived it from `lime:conversations-changed` — `startRename`'s own save path now sets `crumbThread.textContent` directly once `renameConversation` resolves.
- **A pre-existing bug in `isProfileFormDirty()` (LIME-31, not this brief's own code), surfaced by removing `window.confirm`:** it compared every profile field's live value against a `profileOriginal` snapshot without checking whether Profile was even the currently-rendered section. Viewing Profile, switching to Login & security with zero edits, then switching back (or just reopening the modal) read every field as `undefined` — none matching the snapshot — and spuriously opened "Discard changes?" on a completely clean form. Invisible before now: `window.confirm` was globally auto-accepted in every test, and a silent native popup is easy to miss manually too. LIME-26's real `confirmDialog` made it an actual, reproducible modal, which is how a regression test (re-running LIME-31-fix's own suite after this brief's changes) caught it landing. **Flagged to the user rather than silently fixed** (out of this brief's own stated scope — Settings' dirty-check, not conversation actions); the user asked for it to be fixed here rather than deferred. Fix: `isProfileFormDirty()` now returns `false` immediately if `#settings-field-display_name` isn't in the DOM at all, before doing any comparison.

**Archive:** a new "Archived" `<section class="lime-section" data-section-id="archived" data-default-collapsed="true" hidden>` in `index.html`, same row markup/pattern as Starred, reusing `syncSection` unchanged. Two behaviors neither Starred nor any prior section needed:
- **Hidden entirely when empty**, not an always-shown empty-state message like Starred's "Star a chat…" — a new `syncArchivedSection()` toggles the section's own `hidden` attribute based on the filtered list's length, wired into the same three `lime:*-changed` listeners Starred already used.
- **Collapsed by default**, where every prior section defaulted to expanded. The shared collapsible-sections IIFE read a per-section `data-default-collapsed` attribute rather than hardcoding "archived" by name into shared code — `localStorage.getItem(key) === null ? defaultCollapsed : stored === 'true'`, so a section with no attribute (every existing one) keeps behaving exactly as before.
- Archiving/unarchiving needed no new store reads: `listConversations()` already excludes archived by default (confirmed by reading `store.js` directly, not assumed), so All and Starred already drop an archived conversation for free; `includeArchived: true` plus filtering on `membership.archived_at` (the same shape as `starredConversations()`) is all `archivedConversations()` needed.

**Delete:** `confirmDialog({ title: 'Delete "<title>"?', ... danger: true })` → `LimeStore.deleteConversation` → `selectTopOrEmpty()`. The conversation menu only ever acts on `currentConversationId` (there's no per-row menu in this app — the caret lives in the topbar for whichever thread is open), so "if it was open" from the brief's own wording is always true here; simplified accordingly, no extra open-check needed. `selectTopOrEmpty` reads `messageConversations[0]` — already fresh by the time it runs, since `deleteConversation`'s own `emit()` is synchronous (`document.dispatchEvent`, confirmed in `store.js`) and its listener (which rebuilds `messageConversations`) has already run to completion before `deleteConversation`'s returned Promise even resolves.

**`docs/data-model.md`:** Principle 7 (permissions) extended with the concrete UI-level decision — DMs' lack of an owner as the *reason* rename/delete are unavailable on one, not just a UI-layer rule stated on its own; non-owned groups get the same treatment for the same reason; "Leave group" (distinct from owner-only Delete) named as a later brief.

**Verification:**
- `node --check` passes; `lime.css` brace count balanced (512/512).
- **jsdom (real app, zero errors):** menu contents exactly match the brief's three ownership cases (`["star","archive"]` for both the DM and the non-owned group; `["star","rename","archive","DIVIDER","delete"]` for the owned one). Renaming conv-010 to "Trivia Crew": breadcrumb, row, and the store all agree; clearing the name back to empty returns "Jean, Mary & Jimin" (the store's own derived-title fallback). Archiving a DM (conv-001, standing in for the brief's own "Alexi" — no such conversation actually exists in the current seed data, checked directly rather than assumed): leaves All, appears under Archived, which becomes visible and starts collapsed; Unarchive brings it back and the section hides again. Deleting conv-007: Cancel keeps it, Confirm removes it (`deleted_at` set, row gone everywhere), selection moves to the new top of All. `grep window.confirm` clean. **Re-ran LIME-31-fix's own full suite afterward** (not just this brief's new checks) — this is what caught the `isProfileFormDirty` regression above; passes clean now, including the corrected discard-confirmation test (now checking `confirmDialog`'s own DOM state instead of a `window.confirm` mock that no longer exists in the code path).
- **Persistence (snapshot method):** conv-010's cleared name (`null`) and conv-007's `deleted_at` both survive a fresh, separate `JSDOM` load from the saved snapshot; conv-007 correctly absent from `listConversations()` on that reload too.
- **Headless Firefox (Playwright), live measurement:** the Delete menu item's computed text color is `rgb(139, 8, 54)` — confirmed against the token chain (`--bad-text-bold-default` → `--seed-brick-760` → `#8B0836`), not assumed from the class name alone. Menu order and the divider's presence match exactly. The rename input opens with `selectionStart`/`selectionEnd` at `0`/`22` — the entire seed value selected. The confirm dialog's own bounding rect is centered exactly on the viewport center (both axes, to sub-pixel precision); its Confirm button is `rgb(168, 75, 60)` background (`--bad-bg-bold-default` → `--seed-brick-500` → `#A84B3C`) with white text; focus lands on Cancel. The Archived section, checked after a full page reload (not just the in-session state right after archiving) so no stored collapse preference exists yet: visible (an archived conversation exists), `aria-expanded="false"`.

**Gate:** in Firefox — open "Math Teachers NYC" (yours): Star, Rename, Archive and a red Delete. A chat you don't own, and DMs, only get Star and Archive. Rename: the title becomes an editable box with the text selected; type a new name and press Enter. Archive: the chat moves to a collapsed "Archived" section at the bottom; Unarchive brings it back. Delete: a confirm dialog appears first, red Delete button, Cancel keeps the chat. "Reset demo data" and Settings' "Discard changes?" both use the same dialog now. **Open question for the user, per the brief's own prompt:** does DMs having no Delete (Archive only) match your intent?

## LIME-32

**CSS-only, as scoped** — `git diff public/index.html public/js/app.js` empty throughout; only `public/css/lime.css` changed.

**`.lime-composer__box` (shared by both composers):**
- Added `container-type: inline-size; container-name: composer;`. Verified live (Playwright/Firefox): `width: 75%` and the Send button's absolutely-positioned corner anchor are both unaffected — the box already had `position: relative` for that anchor, which already made it a containing block for absolutely-positioned descendants, so `container-type` added nothing new there.
- **A real, corrected assumption:** I first assumed (from general CSS Containment recollection, not measurement) that `@container` size conditions compare against an element's border-box regardless of its own `box-sizing`. Measured live instead — swept `box.style.width` directly and watched exactly when the condition started matching — and found Firefox compares against this element's own **content-box** size (its unchanged, default `box-sizing: content-box`), not the border-box `getBoundingClientRect` reports. Caught before it shipped a wrong threshold: an early version of the rule, `max-width: 400px` derived from a border-box sweep, was collapsing about 26px (the box's own padding+border) earlier than necessary. Corrected to `374px`, measured directly in content-box terms this time — the exact same units the condition itself uses.

**The container query, replacing `@media (max-width: 480px)`:**
- `@container composer (max-width: 374px) { .lime-composer__toolbar .lime-composer__tool--overflow { display: none; } .lime-composer__overflow { display: inline-flex; } }`. 374px measured live: sweeping the box's content-box width and reading the real rendered gap between the last visible tool and Send's own left edge at each step — gap crosses 8px exactly at 374px, and only grows above that.
- **A real bug found and fixed, not just measured around:** the two overflow-only dividers carry both `.lime-composer__divider` (its own `display: block` rule, later in the file) and `.lime-composer__tool--overflow`. Equal specificity (0,1,0) ties, and the later rule (the divider's) won on source order — meaning my first version of the collapse rule successfully hid the 5 overflow *buttons* but left both dividers visibly dangling among the 4 always-shown tools. This is exactly what the brief's own change-item 3 flagged as a risk ("hide `.lime-composer__tool--overflow` **and any divider left dangling**") — caught live via the matrix verification below (`toolCount: 7` instead of the expected `5` whenever collapsed), not assumed safe from reading the CSS. Fixed by scoping the selector to `.lime-composer__toolbar .lime-composer__tool--overflow` (specificity 0,2,0), which reliably outranks the divider's rule regardless of source order — more robust than reordering the two rules, which would have left the *same* kind of tie-breaking fragility for the next person to trip over.

**The reply composer: kept its unconditional collapse, per the brief's own fallback clause** ("if it ever wouldn't [collapse via the shared rule alone], keep the unconditional rule for it and say why") — this isn't a case of the brief being wrong; it explicitly anticipated needing this decision. Measured, not assumed: the right panel's resize handle enforces `MIN_RIGHT = 240` but no maximum (checked `app.js`'s own resize code directly). Sweeping `--right-width` from 240 up to 700 (after first finding and discounting a red herring — `.seed-layout`'s own `transition: grid-template-columns 200ms ease` meant an immediate rect-read after setting the variable was catching the animation mid-flight, not its settled value; disabling the transition for the sweep fixed this) showed the reply composer's own box tracks `--right-width - 32px` linearly, with no cap of its own — meaning at `--right-width ≈ 432px` or more (a real, reachable width on any reasonably wide monitor, not just a theoretical one), this composer's box would exceed the 374px collapse threshold and the shared container query alone would stop collapsing it. Kept `#replies-composer .lime-composer__tool--overflow { display: none; }` / `#replies-composer .lime-composer__overflow { display: inline-flex; }` exactly as they were (unconditional, outside any `@container` block) — both still carry ID-level specificity, so they continue to win regardless of what the shared rule would otherwise do at any given width.

**Item 5, the `#layout:not(.seed-layout--right-hidden) .lime-composer__tool` size tweak (28px tools when the right panel is open) — removed, not trimmed, per the brief's own "only if measurement shows it's no longer needed."** Measured across the full required matrix (below) both with and without it: identical result either way — at every width × right-panel-state tested, the toolbar is either already collapsed by the container query (tool size can't matter; the same 5 items show regardless of whether they're 28px or 32px) or has ≥21px of gap to spare at full 32px size. The tweak's original job — helping the *uncollapsed* toolbar keep fitting when the right panel narrowed the box — is now the container query's job, done precisely, at every width, not just the ones this tweak happened to help with. Left a comment in its place rather than a silent gap, since older comments elsewhere reference "the right panel open" scenario this tweak used to handle.

**A genuine pre-existing gap found while building the verification matrix, unrelated to this brief and left alone:** Seed's own vendor `layout.css` unconditionally hides `.seed-layout__right` at `max-width: 1024px` (only reachable again via `.seed-layout--mobile-right-open`, a floating-overlay class Seed provides for exactly this range) — but this app's own JS never wires that class up to anything. Between 768px and 1024px window width, **the right panel — and so the reply composer — is completely unreachable by a real user**, regardless of anything in this brief. Confirmed live (not assumed) while trying to build a "900px, right panel open" test case per the brief's own matrix: forcing every class/attribute combination the app itself uses still left `#right-panel` computing `display: none`. Not fixed here — out of this brief's own CSS-only scope, and a real product decision (add the mobile reveal-button wiring? just accept the gap?) that isn't mine to make. Flagged here for plot to turn into a brief if it's worth one.

**Verification:**
- Brace count balanced (510/510 — down from 512: two whole rule blocks removed for item 5, net zero elsewhere).
- **Headless Firefox (Playwright), the brief's own full matrix** — both composers expanded and Send active, every width × right-panel-state, reporting collapse state, tool count, the gap to Send, and overshoot (whether anything renders past the box's own right edge):
  - 1567 closed: main uncollapsed (11 tools), gap 371px. 1567 open: main uncollapsed, gap 134px; reply collapsed (5 tools), gap 42px.
  - 1280 closed: main uncollapsed, gap 156px. 1280 open: main **now correctly collapses** (362px box, content-box < 374), gap 136px; reply collapsed, gap 42px.
  - 1100 closed: main uncollapsed (413px box — this is the case the corrected 374px threshold fixed; the earlier wrong 400px threshold collapsed this box unnecessarily), gap 21px — the tightest margin measured, still comfortably ≥8px. 1100 open: main collapsed, gap 136px; reply collapsed, gap 42px.
  - 900 closed: main collapsed, gap 145px. (900 open skipped — the pre-existing gap above makes it an unreachable state, not a real one to test.)
  - 767 (mobile `data-mobile-view`, one view visible at a time as real navigation there works): thread view, main uncollapsed, gap 173px. Panel view (reply composer full-screen), reply collapsed, gap 509px.
  - **Every single case: gap ≥ 8px (minimum 21px), zero positive overshoot anywhere** (nothing ever renders past the box's own right edge).
- **The "…" menu, LIME-20:** opened at 900px (toolbar collapsed) — its dropdown's bounding rect sits entirely within the 900×900 viewport, confirmed by coordinates, not assumed from "it worked before."
- Firefox has supported container queries since version 110 (2023) — noted in the CSS comment per the brief's own request; this session's own Playwright/Firefox testing throughout this brief is itself a live confirmation of that support, not a citation.

**Gate:** in Firefox, open a chat, click into the message box, then make the window narrower and wider. The formatting icons tuck into "…" before they ever reach the Send arrow — precisely, at 374px of the composer's own content-box width, regardless of what's causing that width (window size or the right panel). The reply box behaves the same, via its own unconditional rule.

## LIME-34

**Store (`store.js`):**
